# Core Rules: changes applied to the book, and what is open

The book is built from `rules-src/` with `build_rules.py` (assets in `rules-assets/`). How the files are written is in `rules-src/README.md`; `rules-src/INDEX.md` (generated) is a map of every chapter; `python3 build_rules.py --where Term` lists every mention of a term. The full earlier version of this list was in `archive/old-notes/rules-backlog-history.md` (not in this bundle).

## Open for the book
- [ ] **FOR THE STAGES WORK (Lucas, 2026-11-14): flat values on the redesigned class cards may need to grow by Stage.** First case: Lay On Hands' Guard (2 + your Power Modifier). "Lets not do that yet but keep in the notes for when we do Stages more indepth." Also to look at then: Judge's reduction (the Power Modifier), Binding Light's Holy die, Smite's dice. (The unused AP's Guard already grows: 2 / 2 / 3 / 4.)
- [ ] **THE SENSES RULE, SECOND WORDING (2026-11-15; Lucas found the table "still confusing... maybe a diagram can help"): two questions in order and a diagram of four scenes.** (1) Your eyes: how you perceive your target sets your penalty (Clear none, Obscured 1 Field Bane, Hidden Doomed, Unsensed no target). (2) Its eyes: only if you perceive it better than it perceives you, a bonus (1 Field Boon if you are Obscured to it, Favored if Hidden or Unsensed). No exceptions, so two cases change: each Obscured to the other = 1 Field Bane each; you only hear a creature that can't sense you = a normal roll (today Doomed). The scenes (a torch in the dark; both at the edge of the light; one Half-Blinded; both in the dark) are on the reading page, to be drawn by `book/diagrams/diagrams.py` when applied. Waiting for his word. **Proposed with it: the second Art, Hold the Line** (Brace: until your next turn starts, squares adjacent to you are Difficult Terrain for enemies; Trade Places: you and a willing ally within Reach swap squares, each moved by a Reposition; Haul: Reposition 2 a willing ally within Reach).
- [ ] **THE SENSES RULE REWRITTEN AS A TABLE: FIRST WORDING (superseded by the entry above) (2026-11-15; Lucas asked for a more intuitive wording, and the same rule for Obscured; not applied).** Today (Chapter 3, Senses): a two-column table (your Actions against it / its Actions against you) plus the sentence he finds confusing ("you gain no Favored or Boon from a creature's poor perception of you if you perceive that creature as Hidden"). **Proposed: one table, read by row and column.** Rows = how you perceive your target; columns = how it perceives you. Clear row: normal / 1 Field Boon / Favored. Obscured row: 1 Field Bane / 1 Field Bane / Favored with 1 Field Bane. Hidden row: Doomed in every column. Unsensed: you can't target it. And one line: "How you perceive your target comes first: being hard to see helps you only against a target you perceive better than it perceives you." **The one case that changes:** two creatures each Obscured to the other take 1 Field Bane each (today the Bane and the Boon cancel and they fight normally). Research (`book/research/conditions-and-senses.md`): 5e's cancelling in darkness is a well-known complaint, and "both at a penalty" is its common house rule. Foundry's senses code and test follow when applied.
- [ ] **THE NEW SIGHT CONDITION IS NAMED HALF-BLINDED (Lucas, 2026-11-15: "a long name, but makes sense"); DAZED STAYS.** Was: the name open. Lucas: Dazzled and Dazed are too alike; rename Dazed to Stunned? Claude's answer: keep Dazed (it takes a counted number of AP, which is what the genre calls dazed; "Stunned" carries the hate for losing a whole turn, and Pathfinder 2e's Stunned N is our Dazed N); name the new one Half-Blinded (or Glare-Struck, Bleary). Renaming Dazed would touch Chapter 8, the Glossary, the cards (the Archer's Staggering Shot), the Bestiary's rider tables, Foundry's condition item and the SIM.
- [ ] **THE PALADIN, DECISION 8 DECIDED: LANTERN OF FAITH, THE LIGHT ART (Lucas, 2026-11-15; not in rules-src).** Paladin Art; Round; 1 AP; [Divine]. Kindle: you shed Bright light 3 until the encounter ends. Reveal: one creature you perceive within 5 squares sheds Bright light 1 (1 round). Glare [Power, Save]: one enemy you can see within 5 squares makes an Instinct Save; Tier 1 or 2: Half-Blinded (EoT). **Asked and answered: Obscured stays at 1 Field Bane** (it is two-sided, so already a swing of 2; the Field limit is 2; it matches Low cover on the ladder). Noted: two creatures each Obscured to the other cancel out, so Dim light matters only when it is one-sided.
- [ ] **A NEW CONDITION PROPOSED: DAZZLED (Lucas's idea, 2026-11-15, with the light Art; not applied).** Between normal sight and Blinded (Pathfinder 2e has one by that name). **Draft for Chapter 8 (named Half-Blinded since):** "Half-Blinded. Your Sight perceives no creature better than Obscured: a creature that would be Clear to you is Obscured. Your other senses aren't affected." (So, by the Senses table, 1 Field Bane on its Actions against them and 1 Field Boon on their Actions against it.) Price, a judgment: Moderate (about 12 to 16 a landing; between Frightened and Blinded (EoT)). It would be the 18th condition; the glossary, Foundry's conditions (an Effect item, as the eight of AU part 1) and the Bestiary's rider table follow. **Lantern of Faith's Glare would use it** (an Instinct Save; his three-result line, or "Tier 1 or 2: Dazzled (EoT)": waiting for his pick). He liked the Art ("the type of thing that was missing": in the charter as what an Art is for).
- [ ] **THE PALADIN'S ARTS: THE THEMES DECIDED, THE FIRST PROPOSED (2026-11-15; not in rules-src).** Lucas: light, the line, the word; light first. **Proposed: Lantern of Faith** (Paladin Art; Round; 1 AP; [Divine]). Kindle: you shed Bright light 3 until the encounter ends. Reveal: one creature you perceive within 5 squares sheds Bright light 1 (1 round). Glare: one enemy you can see within 5 squares perceives creatures more than 3 squares from it as Obscured (EoT). **For the Hide rules when they are written (B2): a creature that sheds light can't Hide** (proposed).
- [ ] **THE SYSTEM'S PURPOSE (Lucas, 2026-11-15): "fun, engaging, tactical and having the least amount of ambiguity, one thing that causes the most problems in games I have played."** In CLAUDE.md. A card that needs an undefined word gets the rule written.
- [ ] **"EFFECTS HAPPEN ON TIER 2+" IS REMOVED, BOOK-WIDE (Lucas, 2026-11-15: "Yes"; DECIDED, not applied).** An **Effects** entry is an extra thing the Action does, whatever the roll (as Draw Steel's and 4e's "Effect"). What happens only on some Tiers is written on those Tiers' lines, or as "Tier 1 or 2: ..." (a Tier line can name two Tiers; a Tier that isn't written does nothing). Why (Lucas): an Action can do something real on a Tier 1 without squeezing it into a short line. **Applied in one pass, with the redesigned classes, never card by card. Places:** Chapter 1 (the card anatomy; Saves; Save Actions: "its Effects row happens on Tier 1 and 2", "[Save, Weapon] deals its [Mod] on Tier 1 and 2"); rules-src/README.md (the card table: "`**Effects**` (what happens on Tier 2 or 3)"); every card with an Effects row in `book/cards/*.json` (Basic Actions, Archer, Bestiary, Gear, the set-aside Talents): its text moves to the Tier 2 and Tier 3 lines or gets "Tier 2 or 3:"; `build_rules.py`'s card rendering; Foundry's card text, the `fx` keys (`all`, `t2`...) and the generator (`gen.mjs`); the ruler's "on 2+" lines. **A Tier 1 that does something stays a choice per card** (a Weapon Attack's 0 on a Tier 1 is part of why a class card's Tier 1 feels special).
- [ ] **A RULING TO WRITE: "MOVING AWAY FROM YOU" (Lucas, 2026-11-15, with Rebuke: "we want to make the game having the least amount of ambiguity").** Chapter 2 defines "Moving Closer"; its mirror must answer, in the text: (1) the measure (the distance in squares, every second diagonal 2, larger after the movement than before); (2) an Interrupt checks before the creature enters the farther square, a Trigger whether it entered any farther square during the movement; (3) a step that keeps the distance the same is neither closer nor away; (4) a movement that goes closer and then away (running past him) counts, from its first farther square; (5) Evade, Forced Movement and Reposition never trigger it; (6) for a Large creature, the distance between the nearest squares; (7) "within N squares of you" is checked at the square where the Reaction is taken; (8) a disrupted movement ends in the square the creature was in when the Reaction was taken. With a diagram (Y is you).
- [ ] **THE PALADIN, DECISION 7 DECIDED: REBUKE, THE SECOND HALLMARK REACTION (Lucas, 2026-11-15; not in rules-src).** 1R; Turn; [Divine, Power, Save]; Appeal Save (DC 11 + the Power Modifier). Trigger: an enemy within 3 squares of you moves away from you. The enemy is Hindered (EoT) and makes an Appeal Save. Tier 1 or 2: its Move Action is disrupted. **The Paladin's level 1 is now: Judge and Smite (Features), Binding Light or Lay On Hands, Shield of Faith or Rebuke; the Arts and the Escalation 2 Passives are next.**
- [ ] **THE ADVANCE AND EVADE RULES MOVE ONTO THEIR ACTION CARDS (Lucas, 2026-11-14; DECIDED, not applied).** "Another bad pattern I noticed: the full Evade rules are something general, but we have the Evade Action that lets you Evade. People may look to the Evade Action and think this is the whole action... Lets move the full Evade rules and Advance rules to their Actions. It will be big cards but that's fine." So the Advance card and the Evade card (`book/cards/basic-actions.json`; Chapter 6) hold their whole rules (what the movement is, Reactions, Difficult Terrain, an Evade used as an Advance, no splitting, the Interact inside an Advance); Chapter 3's "Advance" and "Evade" sections keep a short pointer and the three-question table ("Moving and Leaving Your Square"). **Careful:** the pack applying the Advance rule (one piece, Interact during it) edits the same text; do this after it, or in it.
- [ ] **TWO MORE WRITING RULES, WITH REBUKE (Lucas and Claude, 2026-11-14; proposed, not applied).** (1) **"Moving away from you"** as a defined moment, the mirror of Chapter 2's "Moving Closer" (the distance is larger after the movement than before; a Trigger checks whether it entered any square farther from you; Evade, Forced Movement and Reposition never trigger it). (2) **A Tier line can name two Tiers, and a Tier that isn't written does nothing** ("**Tier 1 or 2:** its Move Action is disrupted"), for effects that are yes or no; it answers Lucas's "Disrupted / Disrupted / No effect clutters a lot". **Rebuke, revised proposal:** 1R; Turn; [Divine, Power, Save]; Appeal Save. Trigger: an enemy within 3 squares of you moves away from you. The enemy is Hindered (EoT) and makes an Appeal Save. Tier 1 or 2: its Move Action is disrupted.
- [ ] **THE PALADIN, DECISION 6 DECIDED: SHIELD OF FAITH, THE FIRST HALLMARK REACTION (Lucas, 2026-11-14; not in rules-src).** 1R; Turn; [Divine, Focus]. Interrupt: an enemy's [Strike] Action would damage an ally within 5 squares of you. That damage is reduced by 1 + your Power Modifier, and the enemy is Taunted by you (1 round). The name stays (a flat Defense card, if the class gets one, takes another name). **Proposed next: Rebuke** (1R, Turn; Trigger: an enemy within 5 squares of you moves closer to an ally; the triggering Move Action is disrupted, and the enemy is Hindered (EoT)).
- [ ] **THREE RULES TO ADD WITH THE REDESIGNED CLASSES (Lucas, 2026-11-14; decided, not applied).** (1) **Chapter 2, Setup: "A Setup is optional unless it says you must."** ("Can should be the default, and Must the addition": cards don't write "can" in a Setup.) (2) **Chapter 2, Setup and Targets: the targets are named before the Setup, and the Action's Distance is measured after it;** a target not within the Distance then isn't affected, and the cost is still paid (the Talent Cut Through already works so; the rule isn't written). (3) **Chapter 3, Straight Line: "toward a creature"** = the line goes to the center of the target's space; you can stop in any square on the line and can't enter the target's space. Why: "a clear move, with a clear end point": closer, never around (he doesn't want Pathfinder 2e's Sudden Charge habit of running around a creature to flank). Charge keeps its own stop ("the first square from which the target is within your Reach").
- [ ] **THE PALADIN, DECISION 5 DECIDED: LAY ON HANDS, THE HELP HALLMARK (Lucas, 2026-11-14; not in rules-src).** Paladin Hallmark Action; Round; [Divine, Focus]; Distance: Reach; Target: one willing ally (the Glossary: "You are never your own ally"). **Setup:** You Advance up to 5 squares in a straight line toward the target. 1 AP: the target gains Guard equal to 2 + your Power Modifier. 2 AP: the target gains Guard equal to 2 + your Power Modifier and can use 1 Rally Point (the class's one Rally Point card). **Proposed next: Shield of Faith** (1R, Turn; Interrupt: an enemy's [Strike] Action would damage an ally within 5 squares of you; that damage is reduced by 1 + your Power Modifier, and the enemy is Taunted by you (1 round)).

Tier 1: [R] 1d6 Ho. Tier 2: [V] 1[W] + [Mod] + [R] 1d6 Ho; Immobilized (1 round). Tier 3: [V] 1[W] + [Mod] + [R] 1d8 Ho; Immobilized (1 round). (The name is Claude's pick for "something more divine" than Halting Strike; Lucas can change it.) **Smite's Tier 3: + 1 more Smite die**, and "neither extra die counts toward the 4" (the Tier 3's and the Reaction AP's). **Proposed, waiting for him: Lay On Hands** as the Help Hallmark (1 AP: another willing creature within Reach gains Guard 2 + your Power Modifier; 2 AP: that Guard and it can use 1 Rally Point).

- [ ] **THE PALADIN, DECISION 3 DECIDED: SMITE IS THE BURST, A FEATURE EVERY PALADIN HAS (Lucas, 2026-11-14; not in rules-src).** **Smite** (Paladin Feature; Encounter; 2 AP; [Divine, Signature, Strike, Weapon]; one creature). Smite dice: [R] 1d6! Ho for each Escalation you have, up to 4 dice (1d8!s against a Profane creature). Tier 1: your Smite dice. Tier 2 and Tier 3: [V] 1[W] + [Mod] + your Smite dice. When you use Smite, you can spend your Reaction AP to roll 1 more Smite die; this die is on top of the 4 (5 dice at most). **Lucas's changes to the proposal:** the dice explode, at most 4, and the Reaction AP gives 1 die over the cap (not 2). **Also decided: the Paladin's Hallmark Strike costs 2 AP** ("so it doesnt become a Striker"): its damage about 35 of its 50 points with the rest in a rider, and its Tier 1 still does the rider. **The Hallmark Actions are a Strike and a Help; the Help is named Lay On Hands** (Guard before the hit; its second use may be the class's one Rally Point card); Shield of Faith stays a Hallmark Reaction's name. **It replaces** the Round Smite (1 / 2 / 3 AP) and Holy Wrath; the Bright light on a Smite's target is left for an Art. **For the book when applied:** an Action that reads Escalation without spending it; the Reaction AP spent on your own Action as a card's exception to Chapter 2. About 100 points at 4 dice against the Judged creature (an Encounter Action's price), 118 with the Reaction AP (computed from the dice, not a SIM run). **Next: the first Hallmark Action.**
- [ ] **THE PALADIN, DECISION 2 DECIDED: JUDGE, THE MARK (Lucas, 2026-11-14; not in rules-src).** **Judge** (Paladin Feature; Turn; [Divine]; 10 squares; one enemy you can see). 0 AP: at the start of an encounter, or as a 0R when the creature you Judged goes Down: the target is Judged by you until the encounter ends. 1 AP: the target is Judged by you instead of the one you had. A creature stops being Judged when it goes Down. A creature Judged by you counts as Profane for you. The first time each round it damages an ally within 5 squares of you, that damage is reduced by your Power Modifier. **It replaces Divine Power.** **With it, three rules for every class card (in the charter, section 2):** a class grants an Action directly (no "you gain the X Action" Feature); a later Feature or Passive can expand an Action (one clause a Stage, written into the Action's text, no new trigger); **Flavor describes the Action** ("You...", what is done and seen: his words on the first drafts, "too much like AI text"; `book/cards/README.md`, "Flavor lines"). **For the book when applied:** Chapter 7's Tags gets "an effect can make a creature count as having a Tag for someone"; "Judged" is a named mark like Marked Quarry, not a Chapter 8 condition. **Next: the burst, drafted two ways** (a level 1 Feature; the Encounter Pick at level 1).
- [ ] **THE CLASS REDESIGN: LUCAS'S FIRST ANSWERS (2026-11-14; nothing applied to rules-src).** **The Paladin's numbers, decided:** Weapon +1 / Power +2, fixed (today: +2 and +1, you choose); Health 20 / 10, Rally 6, Speed +0, Heavy, Attributes unchanged. **A direction:** letting allies use Rally Points "is more of a cleric thing": the Paladin can have some, not much (Lay On Hands as a Rally Point every round doesn't fit him). **The charter:** the utility category is named **Art**; an Art at level 1 and another at level 3; two Versions are two uses, never two sizes; a Passive is never a silent bonus; the class's defining pieces as Features every character has, as a try ("careful to not lose the identity"); 3 Encounter Actions and 2 Passives a rung offered; the level 1 burst tested both ways (in a Feature, or the Encounter Pick at level 1). **Then answered (the same day):** an Art has up to 3 uses, to try ("if it feels too much, we remove one"); **no number of a class's own** (the burst uses AP, the Reaction AP and Escalation read, never spent; Escalation as a spent pool was asked and set aside: it would switch the Passives off, as before pack R); **the Paladin's support is a smaller hit or Guard before the hit** (a step is "more a skirmishing thing", ending conditions and stopping the drop at 0 "a healer thing"). **Proposed, waiting for him: the first Feature, Judgment** (the Judge Action on Mark Quarry's frame; the Judged creature counts as Profane for him, and its first hit a round on an ally within 5 squares of him is reduced by his Power Modifier; Divine Power retired). **His rule for this step: readability first** (he couldn't find what a file reference pointed to): the argument goes in the message or on the reading page, never behind a file name. Records: `book/cards/paladin-redesign.md`, `book/class-charter.md`.
- [ ] **THE CLASS REDESIGN, STARTED: RESEARCH, CHARTER AND THE PALADIN'S FIRST DECISION PROPOSED (2026-11-14, branch `pack-paladin-redesign`; nothing applied to rules-src, the cards or Foundry).** (1) **The research on the Paladin's brief** is in `book/research/`: `paladin-roles.md` (the brief in players' words: off-tank, emergency healer, capped nova, the hybrid trap), `paladins-other-games.md` (4e, 13th Age, the Pathfinder 2e Champion and Guardian, Draw Steel's Censor, Nimble's Oathsworn, Lancer, ICON), `frontline-protection.md`, `nova-burst.md`, `divine-profane.md`; the full reports in `book/research/raw/`. **How it was read:** two passes. The first could open only GitHub (rules read in full: Draw Steel, Lancer, ICON, Nimble, Fabula Ultima, the 13th Age 1e SRD, a 4e Compendium crawl). After Lucas opened the environment's network, the second opened the pages and checked every lead: about 250 verified, 50 corrected, 60 not on their page and removed; Pathfinder 2e's rules read from the Foundry system's data. Still leads: Giant in the Playground, RPG.net, Reddit (they refuse automated readers). **Corrected by the second pass:** only four of the Champion's seven causes protect an ally; the Champion is not called dull on its own turn (the real complaint is its one reaction a round); 13th Age 2e kept its Paladin simple on purpose; a per-battle attack's designer target is 1.5 times a normal one (13th Age), a daily 2 times; a protector with no threat is ignored (the Guardian playtest), unless its protection is certain and has a number (the 4e Swordmage); holy weakness is on 7% of Pathfinder 2e's monsters. **What it found against our rules:** nothing makes the Paladin's front line real (no Opportunity Attack at Stage 0, no zone of control); the class has no nova (Holy Wrath 2 = Smite 2 = 50 points) and none at all at level 1; Divine Power is a type gate (0 without a Profane creature); the Hallmark choice splits the class in two; every modern paladin hangs on a mark the player places; Rally Points are the surge / Recovery pattern, and three games make the paladin spend his own. (2) **The charter** (`book/class-charter.md`, PROPOSED; the charter of 2026-10-29 is in `archive/book-drafts/`): the six kinds (Strike, Help, Compression, Rule-bender, Art, Ribbon), the complexity budget by card, the level lists with an Art at levels 1 and 3 (18 cards a class), nine questions with defaults. (3) **The Paladin, decision 1, the class's numbers** (`book/cards/paladin-redesign.md`, PROPOSED): Weapon +1 / Power +2 fixed (a [Signature] divine Strike rolls +2), Health 20 / 10, Rally 6 (+1 if his own Rally Points pay for allies), Speed +0, Heavy. **Lucas's note on the Advance rule (2026-11-14, for the pack that applies it):** an Advance can't be split; you can Interact once per Advance Action, paying its AP as normal; not in a Linked Advance; a movement Trigger is once per Move Action; **a Reaction that disrupts the movement doesn't disrupt that Interact, and the reverse.**
- [ ] **THE CLASS REDESIGN: DECIDED (Lucas, 2026-11-14; MASTER-PLAN item 5).** The Paladin and the Archer are redesigned fully, step by step, in a new chat, after the Advance rule is applied. **Why** (this chat's findings, `book/cards/paladin-analyses.md` and `book/research/ability-scope.md`): every Action is damage, healing or a defense number; the every-round card (Smite, 133 words) is the most complex; the 1 / 2 / 3 AP Versions script the turn ("Advance, Smite, attack"); the class has no utility at all; leaning on the SIM's white-room numbers hid what the table feels. **Decided:** (1) utility abilities are the top priority; (2) an Action has at most two Versions; a power (the utility category, name to choose: not "Power", already a trait) can have more; (3) a Round Action or a Reaction does one main thing and at most one rider (complexity goes to the rare cards: Encounter, Apex); (4) the SIM gives rough anchors (1 Health ≈ 12, a Weapon Attack 25, an unused AP ≈ 12, an Encounter Action ≈ double), table feel and the research decide; (5) **utility outside combat waits for the Skills design** (Lucas: "let's wait for skills"). **The Paladin, in Lucas's words (2026-11-14), the brief for its redesign:** "The paladin is a soft tank, soft support, soft nova class, focusing in divine action against profane forces. The paladin isn't a consistent damage dealer nor can take hits for you, but they can aid you, make sure you don't go down and when they use all they have against an enemy, they can explode before seeing it coming. They stay in front so the squishes can stay back." Before its cards: a big research on how this feels to play (these terms as tactical TTRPG players use them: soft tank, off-tank, soft support, nova, burst, the paladin's place in 4e, Pathfinder 2e, Draw Steel, Lancer and 13th Age; what makes it feel good), compared with our rules, saved in `book/research/`. **Open for the charter:** the kinds of Action (Strike, Compression, Help, Rule-bender, utility power, Ribbon) and how many of each a class gets per level; the category's name; the level lists. What carries over: the rules engine, Foundry's general automation settings, the identities, the cards that work as moments (Beacon and Beacon's Step, Bulwark of Faith, Rebuke, Mark Quarry) as candidates, not as givens.
- [ ] **ADVANCE IS ONE PIECE, INTERACT DURING IT: DECIDED, NOT APPLIED (Lucas, 2026-11-14: "log it as decided").** (1) **An Advance can't be split:** you move once, up to your Speed, and the movement left is lost (today, Chapter 3 "Advance": you can split an Advance through your turn). (2) **You can use the Interact Action during an Advance** (draw a weapon, open a door on the way) without ending it. **Why:** to make movement worth more (Lucas). **Settled (Lucas, 2026-11-14: "both defaults yes"):** the Interact still costs its 1 AP, paid at that moment; a Reaction to movement fires once for the whole Advance, the reactor choosing the square (Chapter 2 "What a Reaction Responds To", as today). **Proposed with it (Claude, 2026-11-14, for Lucas; research `book/research/gear-and-hands.md`):** the rule is written in Chapter 3 "Advance" and on the Advance card, and the Interact card links it in one sentence (a rule is written once); a **single** Interact per Advance, at any point, only in the Advance Action (not Charge, which can't stop; not Evade); drawing and stowing stay in Interact (it is paid, so gear isn't free; Expert Mastery keeps its value); Release (0 AP) needs no rule. **Linked movement (Claude's proposal, 2026-11-14):** the Interact fits only in the Advance Action used on its own: not in an Advance that is a Linked Action ("you Advance 5, make a Weapon Attack (1A) and Advance 5 again"), a Setup ("Setup: Advance 3"), or part of another Action (Charge), unless that card says so (a whole Action can't be slipped inside another Action's sequence). **Bleed is renamed Lingering, and gets its own removal, Shake Off (Lucas, 2026-11-14; the proposals above accepted: "Those are fine yes. The previous message too.").** Lingering because the condition works for any damage type (fire, holy, poison), and "Bleed" for holy damage confuses ("Ongoing" was rejected: it doesn't sound harmful; "persistent damage" is too long). It is written **Lingering [R] 1d6 Ho (Mi/Ap)**; Foundry keeps its internal `bleed` key (no migration), only the words change. Ending it leaves Interact (Lucas: "I don't like the idea of moving and then attempting to remove it") and becomes its own Basic Action **Shake Off** (1 AP, Handle; on yourself or a bleeding creature within your Reach; the same Save and Tiers as today), so it can't be done mid-Advance. The other "help" uses of Interact (help a Prone creature stand, help an Imperiled creature to safety, wake an Unconscious one) stay in Interact and so can happen during an Advance (the default: the cinematic run-past rescue); Lucas can move any of them out the same way. **Places for Lingering and Shake Off:** every "Bleed" in Chapters 1, 8, 9 and 14 and in `paladin.json` (Final Verdict) (about 70 mentions; the Chapter 8 entry moves to its alphabetical place), Chapter 8's removal (line 10: "Interact (1 AP)"), the glossary's Bleed and Interact entries, a new card in `basic-actions.json` (Chapter 6 places it), Foundry's Bleed texts (`fxdetails.mjs`, `fxrules.mjs`, `fxsheet.mjs`, the Bleed condition in `tools/content.mjs`) and the compendium. **Every place to touch:** Chapter 3 lines on splitting an Advance (the "You can split an Advance" paragraph and its Reaction sentence) and Evade's "the way you can split the Advance Action"; the glossary's Advance ("It can be split") and Interact entries; the Advance and Interact cards (`basic-actions.json`); Chapter 2 needs no change. The other mentions of Interact stay (waking, Bleed, Imperiled, Expert Mastery, the Handle trait). - [ ] **THE PC BUDGET: MEASURED, A GUIDE FOR THE CARD SESSIONS (Lucas, 2026-11-13: "this done before the card sessions"; no rule changed; branch `pack-pc-budget`; `book/ruler/pc-budget.md`, the guide in `book/cards/README.md`).** A plain PC (its class's Health, armor, weapon, Modifiers; nothing else) with a turn of three Weapon Attacks is 100 points (a Weapon Attack 25), a PC that deals no damage 0; everything else sits on that line by the party's cost (2,500 fights a level, levels 1 to 3, four Standards with effects; the melee seat is the Smite Paladin's, the ranged seat the Double Shot Archer's). **Found:** (1) **Health is worth 3 to 5 times damage:** 1 Health saved = 5.4 damage dealt in a normal fight, 3.4 in a hard one, 3.1 in a deadly one (the old price list: 1.1); the plain melee PC's whole offense saves the party 13 Health a normal fight (the party's cost 39% → 28%) and the deadly fight is won 72% with it against 41% without. (2) **Shapes:** a 2 AP Action at 50 with a Weapon Attack 106 to 107 (no premium needed), a 3 AP Action at 75 exactly 100, a 2 AP at 60 115 to 117; an Encounter Action at double adds about 30 points to every turn of a normal fight (2 AP: 133 melee, 130 ranged; 3 AP: 122, 140). (3) **The classes today** (levels 1 / 2 / 3): Smite Paladin 115 / 126 / 118, Lay On Hands 126 / 161 / 163, Double Shot 174 / 163 / 168, Rolling Shot 194 / 182 / 200: the Archers get far more from their class than the Smite Paladin. (4) **Riders on an enemy** (the price on an Action; Standards · an Elite): Hindered, Immobilized 0 to 3; Taunted 1 to 3; Frightened 2 to 4 · 6; Vulnerable 1 1 to 3 · 4; **Bleed 1d4 / 1d6 0 to 2** (the party kills its target before it bleeds: 3.8 damage a fight from 5.5 landings); Vulnerable 2 5; Slowed 1 7 to 10 · 12; Prone 7 to 9 · 11; Dazed 1 11 to 15 · 20; Blinded 12 to 15 · 26; Slowed 2 13 to 20 · 26: the guide's ladder is Mild 3 / Moderate 8 / Severe 12 / Extreme 18, one size more against an Elite. (5) **Reactions:** Shield of Faith adds 66 points a turn (it fires in 82% of its rounds), Covering Shot 16 (111%), Rebuke 14 (66%), Overwatch 10 (85%): all over the half-the-rounds target, and Shield of Faith alone is two thirds of a plain PC; the guide: a 1R Reaction about 10 to 15. (6) **Helps (1 AP):** a heal of 1d8 + [Mod] of new Health 69, 2d6 + [Mod] 85 (about 12 points a Health), Guard 1d8 + [Mod] 35 (6 a point), +1 Defense to an ally 12, the unused AP 12, an ally's own Rally Point -11 (it moves Health the party already had). (7) **A ledger** of every class Action's damage in points (Smite 2: 45 for 2 AP; Double Shot 2: 50; Holy Wrath 2: 50 to 54; Rolling Shot 2: 36; Deathmark 65). **Lucas's targets (2026-11-13; in the guide):** a class about **150** (read as level 3's, 130 and 140 before: an assumption); **the Paladins come up and the Archers stay** (first "the middle ground"; rewritten the same day, Lucas: "Yes, rewrite that way": the points are against each seat's own plain PC, and a plain Paladin saves the party 13 Health a fight against a plain Archer's 8; in Health saved at level 2 the builds are Lay On Hands 18.8, Smite 14.7, Rolling Shot 14.2, Double Shot 12.7, so 150 is about 20 Health for a Paladin and about 12 for an Archer, where the Archers already are); **Shield of Faith about 15** (the Lay On Hands Paladin then about 110 to 115, and each Paladin needs about 35 points of new class value); healing: "your choice": its AP stays, a heal is paid with Rally Points, and new Health costs 12 points a Health (a 1 AP Round card about 2). **Added the same day (Lucas: "run the first three now"; `pc-budget.md` sections 7 to 9):** **(8) each Passive alone** (adds, at Momentum 0 · 2): Radiant Zeal 3 · 7, Searing Blows 0 · 4, Reckoning 0 · 0, Font of Mercy 0 · 0, Mending Light 9 · 37, Hallowed Stance 0 · 3; Sharpshooter 4 · 14, Marked Quarry 26 · 39, Killer's Focus 9 · 34, Hunter's Edge 0 · 10, Finisher 1 · 12: a Passive at Escalation 6 does nothing without Momentum, and the Archer's Marked Quarry and Killer's Focus are most of its class. **(9) Areas** (a 2 AP Round area and a Weapon Attack; pushing its own target away always: -16); today's Collision (2 / 1): Push 1 +5 to +9, Push 2 +20 to +25, Push 3 +40 to +46 on the pillars, corridor and close-quarters maps; **higher (4 / 2): Push 1 +14 to +25, Push 2 +47 to +63, Push 3 +85 to +106.** Priced: Push 1 Forced (2), Push 2 Moderate, Push 3 Severe (about the mean of a fight with walls and one without). **Kept today's Collision** (nothing changed; Lucas decides). **(6) Reactions:** a Reaction's attack has a Round Action's points of its cost (1R 28, 2R 55); off the turn: **half** for one on its own being hit (fires in 40% of its rounds; paid: +1.6), **a quarter** for one on an ally or a movement (Hold the Line fires 12%: +0.6; the approach Interrupt 23%: +2.5); a protecting one (+2 Defense to an adjacent ally, 28%) about 0: 2 points; **a 2R Reaction is weak on an NPC** (the kept AP costs more than it gives: -7 to -25): prefer 1R. **(7) Helps:** a Boon on an ally's next attack 3 points, an ally's Basic attack its points, 1 Health healed 1 point; as an Action of its own a help costs the creature an attack and gives less (one Rally among three Standards: the encounter's worth 94 against 99), so the Bestiary says to put it on a Round Special as a rider. **(8) Several targets: half kept** (Lucas's rule); measured, the party spread out, an area within 2 of its target catches 1.5 PCs a use: at half it is worth 83, at two thirds 103 (a question for Lucas). **(9) Grunts keep their Reaction AP** (a free 1R Trigger on eight Grunts +12; paid as a Standard's: -1, even); Minions have none. **(10) The best-turn check:** 4 (or 3) AP, 3 Strikes, each Round Action once, no Encounter Action: within the budget + 15%. **(11) The "how common" ladder** and the worked example (the Orc Warrior). **(12) The example NPCs rebuilt** (each Action shows its points): Goblin Sneak (Rusty Knife; Dirty Trick with a Pressure Bane), Orc Warrior (Cleaver with a Push 1; Brutal Swing with Prone; Hold the Line, 1R), Grave Wisp (Chilling Touch with Frightened; Dread Whisper with Dazed 1, was Frightened), Ogre Chieftain (Club with a Push 1; Overhead Smash with a Push 2, new; Ground Slam a 2 AP Encounter Area 1 Emanation with Prone, was 3 AP; its pay-back (two fifths) on the other two; Rally the Tribe). **The fights with enemies that use effects** (`book/ruler/stage0.md` sections 9 to 12; four Standards built on the budget, one of each: Raider, Hexer, Cutter, Guard): levels 1 / 2 / 3, cost · a PC Down · won, damage only → with effects: normal 27% · 10% → 28% · 7%, 26% · 10% → 26% · 6%, 25% · 8% → 26% · 6%; hard 44% · 26% · 98% → 47% · 26% · 97%, 40% · 25% → 42% · 22%, 42% · 27% → 40% · 18%; **deadly 66% · 49% · 86% → 80% · 59% · 77%, 61% · 48% · 87% → 70% · 55% · 80%, 60% · 48% · 88% → 69% · 55% · 82%** (fights 0.7 to 0.9 round longer: in a long fight the effects snowball; - [x] **(APPLIED 2026-11-12, pack "The Bestiary's turn budget": the entry above.) THE BESTIARY'S DAMAGE AS A TURN BUDGET: BRAINSTORM WITH LUCAS (2026-11-12; "Only apply things when we are done with brainstorming"; nothing applied, nothing measured yet).** **Why:** the ruler's enemies deal damage only (no conditions on PCs, no Reactions, no Setup, no Action without damage), so everything fitted this session is for damage-only enemies; and the Bestiary ties Frequency to AP (1 AP Round, 2 AP Round, 3 AP Encounter), which Lucas doesn't want: "the building blocks make the system, and it needs to be balanced and still permissive for GMs". He rejected "fewer, bigger attacks" (4 AP is there for versatility). **His model:** each Rank has a **turn budget** of damage (his example: 100 for a Medium Standard) that the GM allocates to Actions within caps by AP and Frequency. Today's rows are that already with the Basic attack at 25: 1 AP Round 35, 2 AP Round 55, 3 AP Encounter 87, and the usual turn (the 2 AP Special and two Basic attacks, one with its Bane) about 100. **Agreed so far:** (1) the GM sees the points; a simplified table can come later; (2) price per AP, plus a premium for each restriction (Round, Encounter, costing 2 or 3 AP at once), so a bigger Action is never worse than Basic attacks and over-budget turns stay within about 15%; (3) riders are paid from the Action's share (a flat cost was proposed, so a Basic attack with a cheap rider keeps most of its damage); a Basic attack is not always damage-only; (4) **no limit on repeating a rider:** a rider the target already has is wasted on purpose, it pushes the GM to spread attacks (Lucas); (5) **how common each kind of Action should be (Lucas):** common: damage + a forced movement; common: damage only; a little less: damage + one other rider; uncommon: damage + 2 riders, one a forced movement; rare: damage + 2 riders of any kind; very rare: a unique effect ("the Dragon grabs you and flies up, dropping you to the ground"); at most 2 riders an Action as guidance, not a hard limit; (6) **Setup and Effects need guidance, not exact math** ("No math is going to be perfect... it needs to FEEL balanced. No one is going to look at all of this calculation except us"). **More, the same day:** (7) placement guidance agreed: an effect in **Setup** (it always happens) is paid one size bigger, on **Tier 3 only** one size smaller, on the Effects row or as a rider the normal price; a unique effect as the nearest condition, on an Encounter Action; (8) **non-damaging Actions** (a buff, a heal, a wall) are priced from the same budget; (9) **an enemy's Reaction** (Lucas): "a bit better than 1 AP, since it could use it on its turn, but it can't be too much since everyone has 1R". Proposed: a 1R Reaction's share is a 1 AP Round Action's (about 35 against the Basic attack's 25), and because it fires in about half the rounds a creature with one gives up about half that share from its turn budget (the Bestiary's "one rating stronger when it doesn't disrupt" folds into this). (10) **[Q] 4 AP and no Reaction AP, to make Reactions worth more? MEASURED (level 2, no Momentum, 800 fights; the ruler's PCs spend their whole turn, so without the Reaction AP they never react: the worst case):** today 26% with the Rally Points · a PC Down in 10% / the deadly fight won 86%; **4 AP, no Reaction AP: 40% · 26% / won 61%**; 5 AP, no Reaction AP: 28% · 12% / won 85%. So the class Reactions are worth a step and a half of difficulty, and the Reaction AP is worth about one whole AP. Playtest 2 is why it exists ("Reactions barely existed": the PCs never kept AP for them). My advice: keep 4 + 1; a Reaction gets its worth from how rarely it triggers (the "half the rounds" target), not from costing the turn. **(11) **An Encounter Action is double a Round Action of the same AP** (Lucas: "it should feel big"). (12) Whether Grunts keep their Reaction AP: the ruler answers it once its enemies have Reactions. **Research (2026-11-12; rulebook pages and designer blogs, little of it tactical-TTRPG theory):** *a per-round budget:* Blog of Holding's "5e Monster Manual on a business card" reads a monster's damage figure as a budget for everything it does in a round, Reactions and auras counted one for one; limited-use attacks may go well over it and multi-target ones deal about half. *Limited use:* Pathfinder 2e's Building Creatures has "unlimited" and "limited use" area damage columns (level 1: 5 against 7; level 4: 11 against 18: × 1.4 to 1.6, on a 2-action activity; "single actions should deal much less"); Level Up (A5E): for every 2 points a limited-use ability goes over the turn budget, take 1 off the other turns. Double is at the high end of that range, on purpose. *Conditions:* PF2e: an ability with a significant effect deals the damage of 2 or more levels lower (a step down, like the Bestiary's rating steps today); A5E prices them flat: minor ones (grappled, prone) cost nothing, a moderate one (blinded, frightened, restrained, slowed) the monster's CR in damage, a major one double, more when it hits several: a flat price, as proposed, and the cheapest riders free. *Forced movement:* liked in 4e because the player shapes the field; by itself it "does very little", it pays with hazards, terrain and Prone (EN World); 4e needed something to stop it (here: Steadiness). Here a Push into a wall deals Collision damage, so nearly free, not free. *Pacing of the big Actions:* Draw Steel paces them with a shared GM pool (Malice), "can't be used two rounds in a row", in the place of per-creature Frequencies: noted, not proposed. *Not found:* anything on how often an enemy's Reaction should fire, or on pricing Setup.
(13) **[Q] Collision damage higher? (Lucas.)** Not before the ruler: today it is 2 a square not moved into a wall and 1 to each into a creature; the ruler will price forced movement at that value and at a higher one side by side, and Lucas decides then. **THE BRAINSTORM IS DONE (Lucas, 2026-11-12), nothing applied.** **To do when the brainstorm is done:** the premiums and the rider prices (the Encounter premium looks small today); enemies in the ruler that use conditions, forced movement, Reactions and no-damage Actions, to calibrate them; then the Grunt and the Minion's Defense (the entry below) on those numbers.

- [ ] **[Q] STEEL YOURSELF'S COST, AND WHAT +1 IS WORTH ON EACH SIDE: MEASURED, NOTHING APPLIED (Lucas's questions, 2026-11-12; level 2, no Momentum; Health lost · with the Rally Points · a PC Down · won).** **A correction to the entry below:** the ruler's PCs do use Steel Yourself (their `when self below 35%` turn); that was not the gap. **(1) Steel Yourself cheaper changes nothing measurable.** For the usual party, Smite + 3 Archers and 4 Archers: 2 AP under 35% (today), 1 AP, 2 AP under 50%, 1 AP under 50%, 0 AP under 50%: every cell within noise of today's (Smite + 3 Archers: normal 35 to 37% · 39 to 42% · 32 to 38%, deadly won 52 to 57%); 0.3 to 0.9 Rally Points used on oneself a fight in every case. A PC goes from over half its Health to Down inside one round of focused attacks, so a heal on its own turn comes too early or too late; its price is not what limits it. What the Lay On Hands Paladin's party has is someone else acting for the hurt PC (the heal at range, Shield of Faith on most hits, Mending Light): which part does how much is not split yet (a question for the Paladin's card session, with the Reactions at half the rounds). **(2) +1 is not the same on each side** (1,200 fights; the usual party): **PCs +1 to every Defense:** normal 8% · 22% · 6%, deadly 27% · 51% · 35% · won 93% (today 11% · 26% · 10% and 37% · 61% · 47% · 86%); the Smite Paladin takes 5.2 a turn instead of 6.4 (**-19%**), an Archer 2.3 instead of 2.7. +2: deadly won 97%. -1: deadly won 80%. **PCs +1 to both Modifiers:** 3.1 rounds, normal 9% · 21% · 5%, deadly 28% · 49% · 36% · 95%; the Smite Paladin deals 28.5 instead of 26.1 (+9%), the Double Shot Archer 27.7 instead of 23.1 (**+20%**). +2: 2.8 rounds, deadly won 98%. **The enemies' Defense +1:** the PCs deal about -5%. **Why they differ:** a PC's Guard, blocks and damage reduction take a fixed amount off what gets through, so a few fewer hits is a much larger share of what is left; an NPC's Tier 1 deals 0 while a PC's often still deals something and its rolls carry Boons; and a PC's Modifier is added to the damage of every hit as well as to the roll. **So Lucas's instinct holds where it matters: +1 on a PC (Defense or Modifier) is one step of difficulty** (the deadly fight from 86% to 93 to 95%); only an NPC's Defense rating is mild. Raising the PCs' Defenses is a real lever, to price like a Modifier raise (one at a Stage, not from stacked small bonuses).
- [ ] **[Q] IS IT THE FRONT LINE OR THE HEALER? AND WHAT +1 DEFENSE IS WORTH (Lucas's two questions, 2026-11-12; measured; the Bestiary's note corrected, nothing else applied).** Lucas: the note "a party with a single front-liner counts as one PC less" reads as "front-liners are stronger" and feels bad for who doesn't want to be one. **He is right, and the measurement behind it was misread:** the party that did badly had lost its Lay On Hands Paladin, not its front line. Level 2, no Momentum, normal / deadly (Health lost · with the Rally Points · a PC Down · won): 2 Paladins + 2 Archers 11% · 26% · 10% / 37% · 61% · 47% · 86%; **Lay On Hands + 3 Archers (one front-liner) 13% · 28% · 12% / 47% · 70% · 58% · 77%**; **Smite + 3 Archers 37% · 40% · 39% / 82% · 90% · 91% · 53%**; 4 Archers 37% · 39% · 34% / won 42%; 3 Paladins + 1 Archer 11% · 25% · 10% / won 92%; 2 Smite + 2 Lay On Hands 1% · 18% · 2% / 8% · 39% · 19% · 98%. Three PCs with a Lay On Hands Paladin: a normal fight as four (10 to 11% · 30% · 8%), the deadly one won 51 to 66%; three without: 39 to 40% lost. **So what a party needs is healing in the fight, not a front line. Part of it is the ruler, not the game:** its PCs don't use Steel Yourself on themselves in a fight (a known gap of the plan policy), so a party with no Lay On Hands leaves its Rally Points unspent until the fight ends. To do before reading more into it: the policy uses Steel Yourself, then the same table again. The note in the Bestiary now says only: the CV is for 4 to 6 PCs; with three, hard and deadly fights play about a step harder. **A question for the class sessions:** how much better Lay On Hands is than Smite for the party (the all-healer party loses 1% of its Health in a normal fight). **Defense:** 4 Standards at Defense 11 / 12 / 13 / 14 / 15 / 16 (1,500 fights): 3.1 / 3.2 / 3.4 / 3.6 / 3.8 / 4.1 rounds, the cost 22% / 24% / 26% / 27% / 29% / 32%, a PC Down 6% / 8% / 9% / 10% / 11% / 14%; the Smite Paladin's damage a turn 28.7 / 27.5 / 26.0 / 25.4 / 23.7 / 22.0 (about -5% a point). A point moves the Tiers by 6 to 7 points (Modifier +2: 28% / 45% / 27% against 13, 35% / 42% / 23% against 14), but with the flat weapon damage a Tier 2 is about two thirds of a Tier 3, so the damage moves less than the Tiers. **A Defense step is about a third of a Health step: about 5 CV on a Standard.** The Bestiary said 8 to 10% a point (written before the flat weapon table): corrected to about 5%. Left as "free" in the CV rule; it could count as a third of a step if Lucas wants it exact.

Measured at levels 1 and 2 (Health lost · with the Rally Points · a PC Down · won): 16 Minions 15% · 30% · 6%; 2 Standards + 8 Minions 13% · 28% · 9% and 11% · 27% · 7%; 1 Elite + 8 Minions 11% · 27% · 8%; 4 Standards + 8 Minions 41% · 68% · 47% · 86% (4 Standards: 12% · 27% · 10%; 6 Standards: 40% · 65% · 47% · 87%): worth its 25 in every mix. **(2) Ranged attacks (the fix for "ranged enemies are under-priced"):** an NPC attack that can be made from more than 2 squares away **pays one step of damage rating** (`config.mjs npcRangedStep`; the Bestiary, "Ranged attacks"). Measured: 4 ranged Standards 7% · 25% · 4% in 3.9 rounds (at full damage 18% · 39% · 25%); 2 melee + 2 ranged 7% · 24% · 3%; 6 ranged 22% · 56% · 34% · 96% (at full damage won 56%); 3 + 3: 22% · 50% · 29% · 94%. A little under a melee fight in a PC Down, right in cost; two steps would be too much (6 ranged: a Down in 7%). **(3) Ratings in the CV (the fix for "everything High"):** Health and damage must balance or the CV changes: count the steps above Medium minus those below (Vigor or Resolve alone half a step; a Modifier step half; **Defense free**); each step left over is 5 / 10 / 15 / 30 CV for a Minion / Grunt / Standard / Elite (`config.mjs npcCvStep`). Checked with encounters rebuilt to about CV 400 with the rule (4 Medium Standards: 11% · 26% · 10%): 3 with High Health and damage (390) 11% · 24% · 10%; 6 with Low Health and damage (420) 11% · 30% · 9%; 5 with Low Health (425) 17% · 33% · 17%; an Elite with High damage and 2 Standards with Low damage 12% · 28% · 15%; 2 with Extreme Health and damage and 1 Medium (420) 17% · 31% · 25% (the spikiest). **(4) A note, not a rule:** the CV is counted for 4 to 6 PCs with at least two in the front; a party of three, or with a single PC in melee, counts as one PC less. **Also:** the Roles table's Artillery follows the ranged rule; the difficulty table lists the Minion encounters again.

A Minion acts before a given PC half the time either way; the slot only decides whether they do it together. Squads that always act first: only the all-Minion fight moves (14% · 23% · 1%). With damage × 1.5 in squads of 4: 2 Standards + 8 Minions 10% · 21% · 6%, 4 Standards + 8 Minions 22% · 42% · 26% · 97% (the targets: 11% · 26% · 10% and 37% · 61% · 47% · 86%); × 2: 13% · 26% · 10% and 27% · 48% · 38% · 95%, 16 Minions 25% · 35% · 23% with a wide spread. **So the shared slot is a table-speed rule (one turn for the GM instead of eight), not a balance rule; the Minion's worth still comes from its damage or its Health.** **(2) Party size** (CV 100 / 125 / 150 a PC, in Standards): 4 PCs 11% · 26% · 10% / 19% · 39% · 24% / 37% · 61% · 47% · 86%; 5 PCs 11 to 12% · 24% · 12% / 18% · 34% · 22% / 37 to 39% · 58 to 60% · 51 to 53% · 90%; 6 PCs 13% · 24% · 16% / 24% · 38% · 35% / 39% · 58% · 61% · 94% (the same cost; a Down is more likely with more PCs, a loss less). **3 PCs depend on who they are:** Smite, Lay On Hands and Double Shot: 11% · 30% · 8% / 13% · 36% · 11% / 33% · 63% · 40% · 84% (as four); Smite and the two Archers (one front-liner, no healer): 39% · 44% · 34% / 55% · 61% · 53% · 92% / 82% · 91% · 85% · won 51%: two steps harder. **(3) Ranged enemies are under-priced:** 4 ranged Standards 18% · 39% · 25% (a hard fight; 4 melee: 11% · 26% · 10%); 2 melee + 2 ranged 12% · 30% · 12%; 6 ranged 62% · 97% · 78% · won 56% (6 melee: won 86%); 3 + 3: won 75%. A ranged Standard is worth about 125 to 130 (the plan policy on the open field: no cover to hide behind, which favors them). Speed 7: no change (12% · 28% · 12%). Attacking Ward Defense: a little harder (14% · 30% · 15%). **(4) Ratings (4 Standards):** Health High 12% · 30% · 14% in 4.0 rounds, Low 8% · 20% · 4%; damage High 17% · 33% · 19%, Low 5% · 19% · 2%; Defense +1 or -1: nothing (11% · 27% · 11%, 10% · 24% · 8%); Modifier High 13% · 29% · 14%; Health High with damage Low, or the reverse: about Medium; **everything High 29% · 50% · 39% · 96% (between hard and deadly: about CV 135 each); everything Low 4% · 13% · 0% (under easy: about 65); Extreme Health and damage 43% · 65% · 60% · 83% (deadly: about 150).** **Reading:** the CV holds for 4 to 6 PCs and for an NPC whose ratings average Medium (the Bestiary already says so). It needs three notes to be honest: a High or Low step of Health or damage is about ±10 to 15 CV (Defense is free); a ranged attacker about +25; and a party of three, or one with a single front-liner, counts as one PC less.

- [ ] **[Q] HOW A PC'S DAMAGE GROWS (Lucas, 2026-11-12; discussion, nothing applied; to decide after the two classes' Stage 0).** Lucas: no HP bloat, but progression must be felt; the Master step (15 Mastery, level 8: 1[W] becomes 2[W]) is one such step, so 2[W] stays (two instances, as Chapter 15 has it; with the flat-plus-die draft a Greatsword's Tier 3 is 8 + 2d8). **Research (forum and blog opinion, 2026-11-12):** bloat is Health growing faster than damage, and the two can't be fixed apart (EN World's HP bloat threads); 4e's 2[W] / 3[W] steps are remembered as sound scaling, its complaint was non-weapon powers falling behind [W]; PF2e's striking runes (a second and third weapon die) work but read as a mandatory item; numbers that only rise feel hollow, new abilities that change how you play are felt more (design writing on vertical and horizontal progression); Draw Steel grows mostly through the characteristic (better Tiers) and fixed kit bonuses. **My reading:** keep "a normal fight lasts about 3.5 rounds" at every Stage (the Bestiary's fit already does it), let damage and Health step up together at the Stage boundaries, a few big visible steps (the Modifier track, Master's 2[W]) and new Actions in between.

THREE IDEAS BEFORE THE STAGE 0 ANSWERS (Lucas, 2026-11-12; discussion and two measurements, nothing applied; `book/ruler/reactions.md`, `node book/ruler/sim/reactions.mjs`).** **(1) [IDEA] Fewer damage dice, a narrower range:** a flat part plus one small die per Tier (for example the Greatsword 0 / 3 + 1d4 / 5 + 1d4 where it is 0 / 1d8 / 2d6): one die a hit at most, the same averages; Lucas: "an amazing idea", his worry: too close to Draw Steel (it keeps 2d12, Boons and Banes and a die, so no). He also wants melee with the higher ceiling and ranged near the floor: do it in that same table. **(2) [IDEA] Opportunity Attack out of the Basic Actions (a class Reaction or a Talent instead). MEASURED:** it almost never happens in SIM today (0.00 to 0.05 a fight with the plan; 0.15 a round for a Paladin that reacts to the first trigger; the enemies' about 0), and with nobody having it the fights are the same within noise (normal 12% → 13% of Health lost, deadly 44% → 43%; the pillars 20% → 19% and 58% → 58%). **Who takes the damage is decided by whom the enemies choose, not by the Opportunity Attack:** enemies that spread their attacks: Paladins 34% and 26%, Archers 19% and 20% in the normal fight, 29 / 26 / 23 / 22 in the deadly one (close to even already); enemies on the nearest PC: 47 / 44 / 2 / 7; enemies on the back line: 17 / 13 / 32 / 38, the same with and without it. The Archers are already easy to reach. **What SIM can't show:** its enemies don't fear the Opportunity Attack, so the rule's threat (a GM who doesn't walk past the Paladin because of it) is not in these numbers. **(3) [IDEA] Reactions that trigger too easily. MEASURED** (uses a round the owner was up): Shield of Faith 0.70 normal / 0.85 deadly; Covering Shot 0.73 / 0.79; Overwatch 0.59 / 0.81; Rebuke 0.54 / 0.64; against an Elite and 2 Standards 0.65, 0.69, 0.46, 0.48. The first round counts with no Reaction AP before the owner's turn, so 0.8 is about "every time it can". All four are close to a fifth AP; Rebuke and Overwatch least. **To decide:** whether to tighten them (and to what rate), and whether Opportunity Attack leaves the Basic Actions.

THE STAGE 0 RUN: MEASURED, NOTHING APPLIED (2026-11-12; `book/ruler/stage0.md`, written by `node book/ruler/sim/stage0.mjs`; Lucas decides).** Stage 0 as `book/decisions-s1.md` describes it, run in SIM with the four R builds cut down to levels 1 to 3 (no Apex, no Talents, no Daily; Escalation 1 to 6, start 1 + Momentum, Passives at 2 / 4 / 6). **(1) Against today's Stage 1 rows Stage 0 is too hard:** the normal fight (4 Standards) costs a level 1 party 31% of its Health with a PC Down in 40% of fights at Momentum 0 (today's level 1 party: 14% and 24%); level 2: 24% and 35%; level 3: 22% and 30%. **(2) The row that fits:** NPC Health about × 0.90 for 3.5 rounds; for damage the Bestiary's two targets don't meet (as pack R-bestiary found): × 0.61 to 0.79 for "the fight costs a fifth with the Rally Points", × 0.91 to 1.01 for "a PC Down in 1 fight of 5". **The row the report uses: Health × 0.90, damage × 0.85** (halfway; mine, to have numbers). With it, level 2: easy 6% of Health lost and a Down in 4%, normal 11% and 13%, hard 22% and 26%, deadly 42% and 53% (won 81%). Level 1 is the hard one (19% lost, a Down in 19% in the normal fight at Momentum 0); levels 2 and 3 are about the same (13%, 15%). **(3) Escalation 1 to 6:** with Momentum 0 a fight spends 29% of the PCs' turns at Escalation 1 and only 5% at 6; the Escalation 6 Passive (level 3) works in 5% of turns at Momentum 0, 30% at Momentum 2, 68% at Momentum 4. So level 3 adds almost nothing without Momentum. **(4) Momentum works as a day's reward:** four fights with no Rest (easy, normal, normal, hard): with the ladder (+1 per encounter overcome, -1 per PC Down) the last hard fight costs 20% with a Down in 31% and the day is cleared 96% of the time; with no Momentum 31%, 44% and 91%. **(5) Maps and how the party plays:** the normal fight hardly moves; the deadly one goes from 42% lost on the open field to 53 to 55% in the corridor and among the pillars; a casual party loses 64% there and wins 61%. **To decide:** the Stage 0 row; whether the level 3 Passive stays at Escalation 6; the ranged-against-melee candidates (the next entry).

RANGED AGAINST MELEE: THE CANDIDATES MEASURED (2026-11-12; Lucas: "Do A, B, C and E. D is bad because I dont to limit that much"; `book/ruler/stage0.md` section 6; nothing applied).** Level 2, the Stage 0 row; "as built" = two Greatsword Paladins and two Crossbow Archers; "all Crossbows" = the Paladins with Crossbows too. Health lost in the normal / the deadly fight, as built against all Crossbows. **Today: 11% / 42% against 7% / 31%** (ranged is better; the Smite Paladin deals 23.0 a turn with the Greatsword and 22.4 with the Crossbow, and takes 7.2 against 3.6). **A (ranged weapons one die smaller): 11% / 44% against 8% / 35%:** it hardly helps, the gap is in the damage taken, not dealt. **B1 (+2 damage on a [Melee] Strike that deals damage): 11% / 40% against 7% / 31%:** small. **B2 (a melee PC that ends its turn Engaged has Guard 3 until its next turn): 8% / 34% against 7% / 31%:** it nearly closes the gap by itself. **B1 + B2: 9% / 30% against 7% / 31%: even** (among the pillars melee is ahead: 37% against 42%). **C2 (a [Ranged] Action while Engaged is Doomed): 12% / 42% against 8% / 35%:** small. **C3 (a [Ranged] Action while Engaged draws an Opportunity Attack): 12% / 43% against 12% / 54%:** it turns the all-Crossbow party into the worse one and costs the built Archers almost nothing on the open field; among the pillars it bites harder (as built 50 to 57%, all Crossbows 67 to 76% lost in the deadly fight). **E (carrying both, the Crossbow first, the Greatsword when an enemy is within 2 squares):** under today's rules it is never worth drawing the sword (0 AP: 8% / 35%; 1 AP: 9% / 38%; 2 AP: 10% / 41%; Crossbows only: 7% / 31%). Under B2 + C3 a free switch is the best choice of all (8% / 30%), at 1 AP it is about even with Greatswords only (8% / 33% against 9% / 34%), at 2 AP worse (8% / 38%). The book today: drawing is Interact (1 AP), free once a turn at 8 Mastery (Expert). **My reading:** B2 is the one that does the work (B1 optional on top); C3 is the strong one if an all-ranged party should be the worse party, and it needs care on tight maps; A is not worth touching the weapon table; with B2 in, the switch at 1 AP (today's Interact) is fair as it is. **To decide:** which of B1, B2, C2, C3; B2's number (3 here); the cost of the switch.
- [x] **(Decided and applied 2026-11-12: the entries above.) RANGED AGAINST MELEE: MEASURED, NOTHING DECIDED (Lucas's question, 2026-11-12, before the Stage 0 run).** Lucas: in most games ranged deals less because it is safe; here the Weapon Modifier serves both, so anyone can carry both weapons. **What the rules have today:** ranged weapons deal as much as melee ones (Crossbow 0 / 1d4+2 / 1d10+2 against the Greatsword's 0 / 1d8 / 2d6; Shortbow = Longsword, 0 / 1d6 / 1d10); a Reload is optional (a bonus shot, not a cost); what ranged pays is Banes (2 while Engaged, 1 Field Bane into a melee, Cover from creatures in the line: about 0.9 Banes on an average shot, near 9% of its damage); nothing on the Paladin's cards asks for a melee weapon (Smite is [Weapon, Strike]). **What SIM measures (4 Standards, open field; the same builds, only the weapon changed):** the Smite Paladin with a Crossbow deals the same (24.9 damage a turn that counted against 25.4) and takes less than half (4.2 a turn against 9.0); with both Paladins on Crossbows the normal fight costs 8% of the party's Health instead of 14% and the deadly one is won 83% instead of 73% (among the pillars 87% against 61%); the Double Shot Archer with a Greatsword deals 17 a turn instead of 27 and takes three times as much. The ranged side also shoots in round 1 while the enemies walk; a melee PC spends that AP walking. **So ranged is the better choice for everyone as written, and nothing pays a PC for standing in front.** Limits of the measure: the enemies are all melee, the plan policy (no one holds a line), no ranged enemies, level 1. **Candidates to measure in the Stage 0 run (Lucas decides):** (a) ranged weapons one die smaller; (b) something for melee (a damage or Guard edge while Engaged, or Flanking worth more); (c) a real cost for shooting while Engaged or for being reached (today the Archer shoots on with 2 Banes); (d) class Actions that name [Melee] where they should (Smite with a Crossbow?); (e) a cost to switch weapons, so "carry both" isn't free.

That was measured with one way of playing (the ruler's plan policy: the party focuses fire and walks to the enemy; each enemy strikes the PC it can hit most). SIM-3 measured others: the same fight costs **9%** when the party holds the line, **20%** when it plays casually (no focus fire); and when every enemy strikes the weakest PC, a PC goes Down in **50%** of the fights instead of 21% (the deadly fight is then won 54% instead of 71%). **The question:** should the target describe (a) a table that plays reasonably against a GM who spreads the enemies' attacks [what it is today, my default], (b) a casual table, so careful play feels rewarded, or (c) a GM who focuses fire, so the listed difficulty is the worst case? It decides how the Stage 0 run fits the Bestiary's Stage 0 row. A second point for the Bestiary's text, whatever the answer: a line telling the GM that focusing every enemy on one PC makes an encounter about one step harder.

Today Chapter 3's Cover counts the corners of the target's space that are blocked from the center of your square, drawn from above: a crate or a low wall either fills its square (it blocks every line through it, like a pillar) or doesn't exist. Seen from the side, the same rule gives the answer for a low object: lines from your height to the target's corners (its feet and its head) pass over it or not. **What a rule could say (to write with Lucas):** an object has a height (low: about half a creature; a creature is as tall as it is wide); draw the lines from the side as well, and an object lower than both ends blocks only the lines that pass under its top: a crate between you and a standing target blocks its two lower corners (Medium cover), none against a target standing on it, all four against a Prone one behind it. **Already in Foundry:** the Cover height window (`module/heightcover.mjs`, v0.0.166) computes exactly this side view from the tokens' elevations; the book doesn't say it. **For SIM:** maps have only full-height walls (`#`); a low object would be a new map character with a height, and `gridrules.mjs coverLevel` a second pass from the side. - [ ] **TABLE SPEED: CANDIDATES (Lucas, 2026-11-09; nothing applied).** The cost today: a PC turn is about 3 attack rolls with their damage, a Standard 2 Strikes: about 40 dice events a 4-on-4 round, 140 a fight, before Reactions; 4 AP is four decisions a turn, for the GM's NPCs too. (1) **Cut dice:** the flat Boons, Favored's "any two matching faces" (CHAPTER 1 REVIEW) and named Boons with the Circumstance limit are speed fixes (adopt them for speed even if feel is a tie). (2) **One throw per Action:** the 2d12 and the damage dice of the rows together, read the row the tier gives (a sidebar; Foundry already does it). (3) **The turn as one sentence** (a Chapter 2 sidebar: "Charge the archer, Weapon Attack, keep 1 AP") and **one or two default turns per class** on its page (the ruler has them). (4) **Reactions:** "Reactions?" asked once, when the triggering Action resolves (only Interrupts stop it); the Opportunity Attack narrowed to "leaves a square you threaten" (Chapter 6 note). (5) **The GM's side:** **squads** (Minions, optionally Grunts, of one kind act on one slot, one roll per target); a **default turn line** on every NPC stat block; **average damage** for NPC Basic attacks beside the dice (the GM rolls only to hit; Elites keep their dice). (6) **Shorter fights:** the decided fight (expedition-loop.md) ends the mop-up; keep 3.5 rounds, never fix speed with Health. (7) **Bookkeeping:** Guard from AP as one number (a token per kept AP on paper), one shared Escalation marker, "in practice one Reaction a turn". (8) **Measure it:** the ruler counts rolls and decisions per turn beside DE (target: a PC turn at most 2 rolls after (2), an NPC 1 per Strike); Foundry times turns in playtests (targets about 90 s a PC, 45 s an NPC).
- [ ] **MISSING MECHANICS, PART 2: CANDIDATES (Lucas, 2026-11-09; nothing applied; research on tactical-RPG complaints, mostly forum and blog opinion).** **Already solved by 2d12** (worth saying in design notes): no healer tax (Rally Points; Lay On Hands spends the target's), no fumbles, tiers set damage (one roll per attack, areas rolled once), no save-or-lose (Dazed, Slowed, Staggered; Disrupt only on a Critical), the Belt against consumable hoarding, Grabbed as simple grappling. **Open, ordered by fit (the five picks marked *):** (1)* **table speed as a design budget** and **NPC squads** (the TABLE SPEED entry). (2) **The paper test:** every rule runnable with a pencil in under a minute; watch the Load grid, Flanking's cancellation, Alert's d12; a GM screen page. (3)* **The class-charter check** (book/class-charter.md) against sameness: each class owns a mechanic nobody else uses, a rhythm (nova, steady, builder) and an out-of-combat identity; the ledger shows the classes winning fights differently; not every class the same Frequency mix. (4) **Dissociated martial Dailies:** one sentence of fiction per Frequency in Chapter 2; a candidate to make martial Dailies cost something visible (a Rally Point, or Exhausted 1 until a Sleep). (5)* **Traps as terrain objects** with a CV cost, written as a situation (a trigger; an effect: Hazardous Terrain, a Deadly Hazard, Alert +1; a use: a found trap is a weapon to Push enemies into); spot and disarm as tiered Checks, three rolls only for a major trap, a failed disarm costs time or Alert, never the trigger. (6)* **Flight and falling by reuse:** falling = Collision (2 Blunt Vigor a square, Prone on landing); a flyer is at a Height, a Prone or Immobilized flyer falls (Trip, a downward Shove and the Archer's control ground it); Reach hits Height 1; a Bestiary rule: every flyer has a reason to come down or a way to be brought down; climbing and swimming as Difficult Terrain with a Body Check under pressure (Load matters: heavy armor sinks). (7) **Journeys:** a short daily procedure on the Challenge rule: each PC takes a role (Guide, Scout, Hunter, Lookout), one Check each, results in the systems (a Supply, a shortcut, Alert, Surprise, a Rough Day). (8)* **The companion rule, written now:** a companion or summon never has its own turn; it acts with your AP, its Actions are cards on your sheet, an area hits it once with you (the Steed rule, applied generally). (9) **Elite Resolve** (for the Bestiary review): once a round an Elite may raise a Save one tier, at a cost (its Escalation, or its Reaction); never a blanket immunity. (10) **Party size:** Challenge progress 1.5 × the PCs; exits never fewer than the PCs minus 2; the Circumstance cap limits flanking with 6 PCs; the ruler runs 3 and 6 PC parties. (11) **Retraining:** at each level-up swap one Talent or Pick; Hallmarks, Attributes and the Health side stay, except in a tutorial window (levels 1 and 2; with the STAGE 0 candidate, all of Stage 0). (12) **The Opportunity Attack** trigger "leaves a square you threaten" (consistent with Flanking); the positions model measures how often movement is punished. (13) **Support:** every support class keeps an offensive rhythm (the Paladin's Smite; a future pure support keeps a Strike line).
- [ ] **ARMOR: CANDIDATE (Lucas, 2026-11-09; nothing applied; fills Chapter 15's "not written yet" armor box).** +1 AD is about -14% Health taken a fight (the ruler), so **AD stays 11 / 12 / 13 / 14 (Unarmored to Heavy) and nothing raises it** (the economy's narrow +1 Warded exception aside, capped by Stacking); armor gets interesting on axes the rules already have. (1) **Weight:** Unarmored 11, Evade +1 square, swims and climbs freely; Light 12, Evade +1 square; Medium 13, Hearing reaches you from 15 squares instead of 10; Heavy 14, **Steadiness +1**, Evade at most 1 square, Hide takes 1 Bane, sinks in water. No Speed penalty (Speed is worth 0 DE on the ruler's field). (2) **Make:** Resist 1 against one subtype (2 for Heavy): padded Blunt (Light), leather or brigandine Pierce (Light or Medium), mail Slash (Medium), scale or lamellar Pierce (Medium), plate Slash 2 or Pierce 2 (Heavy); once per Action, so it is real against the subtype you prepared for (rough estimate: -15 to -20% of matching attacks' damage, a third of the time); enchantments add to it (Resist Fire on mail, Warded +1 WD). (3) **Donning and sleeping:** Light an Interact (1 AP); Medium a Breather, or all your AP for two rounds in a fight; Heavy a Breather, never in a fight; **sleeping in Medium or Heavy: Exhausted 1** (gone at a Sleep out of armor); with Alert's interrupted Sleep, you fight in what you slept in. (4) **Shields:** **Block** (a Reaction: reduce a Strike's damage; the Bulwark Shield of the economy design); **Splinter** (once an encounter, no AP: a Strike that would hit you hits the shield instead, no damage, the shield is Dulled until Repaired; Riders still happen; spare shields cost Load and +1 AP to draw from the pack). (5) **Optional notch** (test last): once an encounter, reduce a hit by 3 × the Armor value (Heavy 9, Medium 6, Light 3), the armor loses 1 Armor value until Repaired. Covers the box's Resist by subtype, drawbacks (Noisy, Bulky reworked), Warded, shields, and starts the unarmored Talent tree. **The ruler checks:** Resist 1-2 by Make against the Bestiary's subtype mix; Heavy's Steadiness against how often NPCs push PCs; Light's Evade for the Archer; Splinter's worth against Repair. Research: Pretendo Games, EN World and RPGGeek on AC vs reduction, Harrigan's Hearth on trade-offs, "Shields Shall Be Splintered" (r/osr, Paizo), Draw Steel's kits.
- [ ] **TALENT STRUCTURE: PROPOSAL (Lucas, 2026-11-09; nothing applied; goes with roleplay-skills.md proposal 3).** Lucas's question: categories per level (today) or Talent points, with the fear of powergamers hoarding. **Research:** PF2e's separate skill-feat pool works for parity, but its best skill feats are combat buffs in disguise and many read as permission; one currency for everything gets min-maxed (point-buy, GURPS, Fuzion's second pool); FFG Star Wars' trees have filler and steep XP; WFRP 4e's XP is "a shopping list"; Savage Worlds' advance (a Major Edge, or two Minor, or a Minor and a skill); Lancer's ranks I-III with redistribution. **Proposal:** (1) **Two tracks, never competing:** each level one Talent (Combat, Mobility, Magic or Class) **and** one Skill pick (a skill rank or a Skill Talent); **Skill Talents never give combat numbers** (no Boons on Strikes, damage or Defense); the permission rule (roleplay-skills.md item 1). (2) **Minor and Major:** a level's Talent is one Major or two Minor; the ruler sizes them (Minor about 0.2-0.3 AP a turn, Major 0.4-0.6, a rung 1 Passive); 2 points a level, spent at the level-up, at most 1 carried over (no hoarding, no shopping list). (3) **Ranks I-III:** a pick can raise a Talent you have instead (rank II from level 4, rank III from Stage 3: renumber with the STAGE 0 candidate); chains (Brawler, then Kickboxer) become ranks or stay Prerequisites. (4) **Stage Talents stay** as milestones (Major or larger). (5) **Retraining:** swap one Talent or one Skill pick at each level-up. (6) **Avoid:** one currency for skill and combat, trees with filler, XP per session as the engine, Skill Talents with combat numbers. **Minimal fallback:** today's rule plus only the separate Skill pick and retraining one at level-up.

- [ ] **STAGE 0 AND 15 LEVELS: CANDIDATE (Lucas, 2026-11-11; nothing applied; ANSWERED the same day in `book/decisions-s1.md`: Stage 0 yes, cap 15 with only Stages 0 and 1 written, option A, Lucas's own level lists, no Attribute increases, swaps on a Rest).** Lucas: "too much starting things, it's hard keeping track of it all"; his idea: a **Stage 0** (levels 1-3), max level probably **15**, then 4 levels per Stage (**Stage 1** levels 4-7, **Stage 2** 8-11, **Stage 3** 12-15). **The problem, counted:** a level 1 Paladin takes 10 things (2 Features, a Hallmark Action, a Hallmark Reaction, an Encounter Pick, 3 Escalation Passives, the Apex, a Talent) and levels 2-4 give almost nothing (3 Talents, a Daily Pick). **Proposal (Claude): Stage 0 as a ramp, one new kind of thing a level:** L1 the 2 Features, the Hallmark Action, the rung 1 Passive, a Talent (5); L2 the Hallmark Reaction, the rung 2 Passive, a Talent; L3 the Encounter Pick, the rung 3 Passive **and the Apex**, a Talent; L4 (Stage 1) the Stage Talent, the Daily Pick, the Stage numbers (with the expedition loop's Escalation 1-6 candidate: rungs 2 / 4 / 6). The class is whole at L1 (Smite or Lay On Hands is the fantasy); without a Hallmark Reaction the Reaction AP still becomes Guard (players learn Guard first, Reactions second); the Apex becomes a goal. Goes with the Chapter 5 Quick Build candidate. **The decision: (A, recommended) Stage 0 has its own numbers and a Bestiary row** (its PCs lack Passives, so the ruler fits a weaker row; every Stage keeps "Stages change the numbers"): Stage 0 the class values; the Health steps at Stages 1, 2 and 3; Guard per unspent AP 2 / 2 / 3 / 4; Rally + 1 / 2 / 3 / 4; an Attribute +1 at Stages 1, 2 and 3; the Modifiers +1 at Stage 3 (the +4 cap holds: still three raises); **or (B)** Stage 0 shares Stage 1's numbers (no new row, but 7 levels with no number change and CV drift inside the Stage). Stage Talents at levels 4, 8 and 12. **Cautions:** level 15 is 50% more class content than 10 for every class (levels 1-4 are all that is written today), and 4 Bestiary rows per role; the 5e trap (tables skip fragile, thin levels 1-2: from memory, not researched): Stage 0 must be **fast (about a level a session) and complete, not weak**. **Middle path:** adopt Stage 0 now, set the cap at 15 but write to 11 (the end of Stage 2); Stage 3 later changes nothing. **Would touch:** Chapter 5 (the Stages table), the glossary, Guard (Chapter 2), Rally Points (Chapter 4), every "Stage 1 = levels 1-4" line, the class level lists and stat blocks (Stage steps), the Bestiary's Stage rows (Health, Basic attack, CV), the Stage Talents; the ruler (the class rung budgets by level, rclasses.mjs at levels 1, 2 and 3, a Stage 0 Bestiary fit); Foundry (the builder, the Stage lookup, config.mjs's Bestiary tables, the migration).
