- [ ] **THE EXPEDITION LOOP: CANDIDATE PROPOSAL (Lucas, 2026-11-09; `book/expedition-loop.md`; nothing applied; the ruler checks it first).** (1) **Escalation 1 to 6** (Passives at 2 / 4 / 6, the Apex at 6), a start at **1 + Momentum**, +1 a round (Lucas's idea); **Momentum** the party's, at most 3, +1 per encounter overcome (fight or not), -1 per Sleep, -1 when a PC goes Down, lost on a Retreat, cleared at a Rest. (2) **Alert** per inhabited site, 0 to 6, visible: +1 per fight, +1 per escaper exactly, +2 per Sleep inside, a site sheet with thresholds (Wary / Alert / Lockdown); a Sleep inside rolls 1d12 against the Alert (found on equal or less), watches and barricades help, the first Breather after a fight is free; fights draw neighbors at Wary from round 3. (3) **The decided fight** (half the enemies' CV lost, or the leader Down, or the GM): runners head for exits (crowded exits give 1 Boon; with one exit the farthest runner surrenders); **one Parting Action per PC** in the players' order: Run Down, Cover an Exit, Call Out, Offer Terms (one PC, all-or-Minor surrender), Shadow (let one go for the preparation bonus); escapers +1 Alert each, runners drop their loot, the encounter counts for Momentum; prisoners give the preparation bonus, -1 Alert or a ransom; two Parting Actions at Escalation 4+; class and Talent hooks. Dropped: Tend, Grab It, Bar an Exit (the reasons are in the document).
- [ ] **DESTRUCTIBLE ENVIRONMENT: CANDIDATE (Lucas, 2026-11-09; nothing applied; fills the "Targeting walls and objects (and the Breach trait)" box in Chapter 3).** Research: object hit points go unused (5e's object AC / HP table is optional; Pathfinder's hardness / HP / Broken is more bookkeeping; the variant people like is a damage threshold); Draw Steel breaks objects through Forced Movement (a cost in squares by material, damage by material); XCOM: destroying cover becomes a tactic and fights leave a mark (criticized when results are inconsistent); Lancer's soft cover (hides, doesn't stop shots) vs hard cover; fire: few real rules, the classic error is a fixed-area spread (big fires grow slower: the Alexandrian), smoke is the real danger, fires burn out, Flash Point is praised because you see where the fire goes. **The candidate:** (1) **Materials with one Break number** (the Vigor damage one Action must deal, after Resist, to break one square; below it nothing happens: no object hit points): Thin (paper, canvas, a plank partition) Break 5, Weak Fire 5, Soft; Wood (a door, a plank wall, a crate) 10, Weak Fire 5, Resist Pierce 5; Thick (brick, a reinforced door, a barricade) 20, Resist Pierce and Slash 5; Stone 40 (siege only); Metal 40, Resist 10. A broken square is rubble (Difficult Terrain) and opens Line of Effect (Cover recalculates by itself). Optional: a hit of half the Break cracks a square (a token), and a cracked square breaks at half. (2) **Objects take only Vigor damage** (Lucas: Resolve is fear and pain; a door has none); **any Fire damage, from either pool, sets a flammable square alight**; creatures that are objects in fiction (animated armor, golems) stay creatures with their own Resolve. (3) **Soft squares** block sight, not attacks: a creature behind one perceived another way (Hearing: Hidden) can be targeted, Doomed, and the wall takes the Action's damage too (a Break check). (4) **The Breach trait:** a Breach Action that breaks a square continues to the creature right behind it in the same Action (the wall subtracts its Break from the Vigor part only; **the Resolve part reaches the creature untouched**). (5) **Forced Movement through walls:** if Collision damage against an object reaches its Break, the square breaks and the creature keeps moving (through a screen, out of a window into a Deadly Hazard). (6) **Fire you can see coming:** a burning square is [HT: Entering, Turn] Fire; at the end of each round each burning square ignites one adjacent flammable square the GM marked a round before ("catching"), so big fires grow faster and players can act on the path; a square burns 3 rounds, then is charred; squares next to the fire are Obscured, and from the fire's second round a creature ending its turn in smoke is Sickened 1; fire is Alert +1. (7) **Collapse:** a support (pillar, beam) is a terrain object with a Break; when it breaks, the area it held is Hazardous Terrain (Blunt) for one turn, then rubble (a terrain object with a CV cost). (8) **Cover that wears down:** Areas and Breach Actions that hit a creature in Cover also check the covering square's Break (ordinary Strikes don't: no extra rolls). Limits: stone and metal hold (breaking them is a Project or a siege); the ruler's map model has no walls (GM judgment until it learns them). Foundry: walls with a material flag, damage or Collision checks it, deletes the wall and drops a rubble tile; fire as a hazard tile with the "catching" mark; smoke as an Obscured region.
- [ ] **HEARING THROUGH WALLS: CANDIDATE (Lucas, 2026-11-09; nothing applied; Chapter 3, Senses; goes with the destructible-environment candidate's materials).** Lucas worried the rule (Hearing: creatures making noise within 10 squares are Hidden, even through walls; creatures in combat make noise; Hide stops it) could upset players. Research: the core is mainstream (PF2e: hearing is an imprecise sense, so an unseen creature you hear is hidden, not undetected, unless it Sneaks; 5e's common reading: your position is known unless you hide, with many GMs giving only a direction when it is not close); "combat is loud" is the consensus, but walls and doors matter; the real complaint is information (on VTTs, seeing enemy positions behind walls spoils ambushes). In 2d12 the rule is good for blind and dark fights (Hidden, not untargetable), ambushes still work (Hide, and Surprise needs every enemy Hidden or Unsensed), and the friction is exact squares through thick walls (untargetable anyway: no Line of Effect). **The candidate:** (1) **Walls by material** (the destruction table): through open space, a Soft wall or an open door, the square (Hidden) within 10 squares; through Wood (a closed door, a plank wall), the square within 5; through Thick or Stone, only the direction and roughly how far (no square: not Hidden, Unsensed for targeting); through Metal or deep stone, nothing. (2) **Text:** "a creature that acts on its turn makes noise until it Hides" instead of "creatures in combat make noise"; and say once that Hidden by Hearing gives the square, not what the creature is or does. (3) **Alert:** neighbors that hear a fight join it ("fights draw the next room" gets a rule: a thick door is why the next room didn't come, an open archway why it did). (4) Fits the armor idea (Medium heard from 15 squares, Heavy takes a Bane to Hide). Foundry: a hearing detection mode through walls, its range from the wall's material flag (the same flag as destruction).

- [ ] **[Q] PACK AU, part 2 (2026-11-10, v0.0.320, Foundry only; no rule changed): two questions for Lucas, each with what Foundry does now.** (1) **The Elite's "first attack each round"** (14-bestiary.md, Elite Escalation): the book doesn't define an attack. Foundry counts any Action that rolls against another creature (a Strike, a Power Action against WD), not a Check, a Save or an Action on itself, and "each round" is the Elite's turn-cycle, as every "first ... each round" in Foundry. (2) **"Round Special Action twice"** and **"Encounter Special Action back, once"**: Foundry reads them as every Round (or Encounter) Action of the Elite that isn't a Basic Action (an Elite with two Round Specials can use each twice), and "back, once" as a second use at Escalation 3 (whether or not it was used before 3). If the Elite should have only one Special at a time doubled, or Momentum-like timing, say so.

(3) **Blinded's "any other Roll that needs sight is Doomed"** isn't detected (Foundry can't tell which Checks need sight): by hand. Nothing to change unless you want a "needs sight" trait.

- [ ] **MODIFIERS, FLAT BOONS, FAVORED'S DOUBLES: DECIDED IN THE BRAINSTORM (Lucas, 2026-11-08; the b/b and Favored parts APPLIED to the book on 2026-11-08, see "B/B APPLIED" above; the Modifiers not yet; Lucas: "not applying anything until we have something concrete").** Takes the CHAPTER 5 REVIEW candidate (1) and the CHAPTER 1 REVIEW candidates (1) and (2) below. **The Modifiers draft for review: `book/modifiers-mod.md` (pack MOD, 2026-11-09; nothing applied; 7 questions with defaults in its section 12; the NPC Modifiers proposed as role values that keep every number).** **Decided:** (1) **Weapon and Power Modifiers are class values**, not Attributes; the ÷ 2 + 1 formula goes. **Track A2:** level 1 the class pair (+2 / +1), Stage 2 +1 to either (+3 / +1 or +2 / +2), Stage 3 +1 to both, never above +4. **Ward Defense stays 11 + Power Modifier (B1)**: a weapon-first PC has WD 12, on purpose. **Classes can be asymmetric:** the Paladin +2 / +1 either way round; the Archer fixed, Weapon +2 (Power +1 assumed). Attributes keep Checks, Saves, Vs. Attribute, Initiative, Wound rolls, the Attribute Actions (Shove, Trip, Haul...), Load and the economy jobs: **the Attributes and Skills chapter is to be written** (with D3, `book/roleplay-skills.md`). (2) **Boons and Banes are flat +1 / -1** (no 1d3-1); **no overflow** (a Boon past a limit gives nothing, not Favored); every Boon and Bane in the book is to be sorted into Circumstance (limit 3), named with its own limit, or unnamed (the NAMED BOONS AND BANES candidate below; the first inventory: the session report of 2026-11-08). (3) **Favored: any two matching faces of the 3d12 are Doubles** (keep the two highest; Several Targets: ignore the lowest die), **three of a kind is a Critical** (Tier 3) whatever the total; Doomed still has no Doubles. **The ruler (2026-11-08, scratch run of the four R builds at level 1, 4000 fights a cell, the new dice switched into calc.mjs for the run only):** the dice changes move nothing beyond noise (4 Standards: Health lost 10.2% → 10.6%; 6 Standards 35.8% → 36.9%, won 82% → 81%); Favored at +0 / +2 / +4 vs DC 13: Tier 3 35.8 / 48.6 / 62.4% → 39.2 / 52.5 / 66.6%, Critical 7.6 / 11.0 / 15.2% → 9.3 / 13.1 / 17.7%; flat vs dice Boons within 1 point at 1 to 3 Boons. **The Modifiers' cost is in the Lay On Hands Paladin, not the Smite one:** Lay On Hands and Shield of Faith add the Power Modifier, so a weapon-first Lay On Hands Paladin heals and blocks 1 less (6 Standards with 1 in 3 Vs. WD NPCs: Health lost 39.8% → 57.9%, won 79% → 63%); a weapon-first Smite Paladin with a faith-first Lay On Hands Paladin costs about nothing (42.5%, 76%); a faith-first Smite Paladin deals about 7% less; the Archer's +1 Power costs only its WD (+1 point of Health lost in Vs. WD fights; no Archer card uses the Power Modifier). **Open:** the Lay On Hands trap (a book note "this Hallmark wants Power high", the Quick Build default, or the cards on the Signature Modifier: card by card with Lucas); the Archer's second value (+1 or +0: today it only sets WD); the Boon inventory's questions (session report). **Later the same day (Lucas, 2026-11-08):** Lucas writes **"b/b" for "Boons and Banes"** in chat and notes (shorthand only, never in the book's text). The Lay On Hands trap is fine as is (neither Lay On Hands nor Shield of Faith rolls, so a Signature Modifier would change nothing); the Archer's second value is +1. **Naming b/b with a limit:** the Circumstance group is renamed **Field** (to try); being discussed: one limit for every named kind (2 or 3; with 2: Cover Low 1 / Medium 2 / High Doomed, Flanking up to 2, a Two-handed weapon in one hand Doomed instead of 3 Banes), the name of the group of Banes the party puts on enemies ("Pressure" feels odd to Lucas), whether spells share it or get their own (Magic) group, Marked Quarry + Hunter's Focus (2 Boons, kept unnamed?) and Banes taken by choice (outside every limit?). Talents are left aside for now. **Decided next (Lucas, 2026-11-08):** **one limit for every named kind: 2** (Flanking up to 2; the "Doomed ladder" was only how two rules that gave 3 Banes would be rewritten, not a general rule: corrected the same day, see below); the enemy-effects group is **Pressure** (kept as the best name so far); **groups go by what the b/b does, not its flavor** (a spell's zone and an arrow's suppression are both Pressure; no Magic group). Next: the inventory of every b/b in the rules (not the classes) sorted into the groups. **The inventory sorted (Lucas, 2026-11-08):** the limit of 2 **doesn't apply to conditions** (their value is their limit: Vulnerable 3 stays); **Prone is Field**; Boons allies give are **Support** (limit 2), **but not all of them** (team play must stay rewarded; proposed: a Boon given to several allies at once, such as an aura, a zone or Rally the Tribe, is Support; a Boon one ally gives one ally with its own Action stays unnamed); Charge's Boon, the Elite's Escalation 1 Boon and Reload's 2 Boons are **unnamed**; the At-will Bane gets no name ("Repeat" dropped: one rule, capped by itself; unnamed); **Pressure** kept. Proposed with it: a name only where several sources share a limit, so Engaged (one rule, 2 Banes) is unnamed too. The groups: **Field** (range, Long vs adjacent, into a melee, Cover Low 1 / Medium 2 / High Doomed, Obscured, Prone, Flanking up to 2, Cover on Saves), **Pressure** (Banes others put on you: Ringing Blow, the Mild "1 Bane on its next roll", the class zones later), **Support**; conditions by value; unnamed (Engaged, the At-will Bane, Lethal / Nonlethal by choice, Charge, Reload, the Elite's Boon, single-ally Boons); Grip: see below. **Correction (Lucas, 2026-11-08):** past 2 Field b/b nothing happens (no Doomed / Favored); only High cover needs a new value: **decided (Lucas, 2026-11-08): High cover makes the roll Doomed, and that is all it gives: no Field Banes from the Cover on top (not "2 Field Banes and Doomed", only Doomed)**; Low cover 1 and Medium cover 2 Field Banes as before; other Field b/b (range, Prone, Obscured...) still apply with High cover (assumed). **Draft text for review: `book/boons-banes.md` (2026-11-08)**. **Round 2 (Lucas, 2026-11-08):** **range Banes have no name** (far 1 / maximum 2 stack with Cover); the "No name" list and its example cut (just "unnamed b/b stack"); "and it is not a Strike" removed from Save Actions (a future Save Action may be one); the Flanking cancels are subtracted first, then the limit of 2; High cover makes a Save Favored; Pressure applies to any roll the effect names, Saves and Checks included; and a writing rule: don't repeat a rule already in place (rules-src/README.md). **Names are fixed, never conditional (Lucas, 2026-11-08):** a Support Boon is always a Support Boon; the Action or rule writes the name, nothing is classified at the table. **The b/b ceiling (Lucas, 2026-11-08):** after the name limits, **reaching 5 b/b makes the roll Favored (Boons) or Doomed (Banes)** instead (in effect a flat cap of 4). **Decided:** the 5 is the net, after the name limits and after cancelling (8 Boons and 3 Banes = 5: Favored; 6 Boons and 2 Banes = +4); **Favored and Doomed from their own sources stay separate** (option B, "for now": they don't count as 5 b/b, so a Favored roll can still have Boons or Banes), and Grip, being unnamed, can keep its 3 Banes.

- [ ] **BASE BUILDING: PROPOSALS (2026-11-07; `book/base-building.md`; Lucas: likely done much later; nothing decided).** Research on D&D 2024 Bastions, MCDM's Strongholds & Followers and Kingdoms & Warfare, PF1e / PF2e kingdoms and downtime buildings, Forbidden Lands, Blades in the Dark, Pendragon, OSR domain play, Draw Steel's followers and the video-game hubs. The complaints (paperwork, no link to the characters, solves no problem, too late, power and money leaks, stale events) and what people love (people more than bricks, something to lose, visible progress, a real benefit, abstraction, time passing). The proposal: a base that solves the economy's real problems (Rest, Load, Supplies, Upkeep and Repair, Purse, Wounds, Retreat, crafting) with no combat numbers; a named person per facility; progress through the story; one Holding sheet handled when you come home; Upkeep and Dulled reused; the base grows into a town with its own town stat block; a telegraphed Threat track; modest income inside the treasure table; early and optional (a rented room at Stage 1); party-owned by default. Six questions at the end of the document.
- [ ] **CHAPTER 13 (ARCHER) REVIEW: CANDIDATES (Lucas, 2026-11-07: logged to review later; nothing applied; card changes go card by card with Lucas, book/cards/README.md).** From the 24 cards, the ruler's ledger (rclasses.md section 7) and a comparison with 5e's Ranger (Hunter's Mark) and PF2e's Ranger. **Kept as good:** Marked Quarry is free (chosen at the start, 0R on Down, 1 AP to switch: 5e 2024's bonus action + concentration tax avoided) and fixes whiffing on the quarry (1 Boon, [Mod] on a Tier 1); two playstyles (Double Shot, Rolling Shot) and two support Reactions (Overwatch 1.07, Covering Shot 1.37, both on target); a varied Repertoire (control, areas, burst; Volley, Deadeye, Rain of Arrows, Killer's Focus, Finisher on target); Thrill of the Kill fits the hunt. **Neutral:** four Passives unmeasured (Harrying Shots, Vanish, Sure Step, Staggering Shot: positional or conditional); much of the class hangs off Marked Quarry (several features are off between quarries); Medium armor and Speed +1. **Candidates:** (1) **A ledger pass, card by card with Lucas:** first the Apexes (Deathmark 1.24, Open Season 0.69: under, partly priced, but the class's peak should feel like one); the 3 AP Hallmark Versions (Double Shot 0.78, Rolling Shot 0.76: the 2 AP Version + a 1 AP Weapon Attack beats them, so they are traps) get more payoff; Twin Shot (2.76, a 1 AP Encounter on two targets, the class's strongest) down a little or its extra dice on the second target only; Hunter's Edge (0.44) up to rung 2's 0.6-0.8; Covering Fire (0.82), Marksman's Shot (1.15) and Hunter's Focus (0.21, partly priced) checked with the positions model first; Pinning Shot a little over (2.10). (2) **Measure the four unmeasured Passives** with the positions model, or a playtest note. (3) **Sharpshooter without the Hallmark choice** (the level list has it; keep the Mastery and the Bane rule). (4) **One grounded out-of-combat Feature** (roleplay proposal 11: reading tracks, where game runs, an eye for an ambush spot; better odds, no certainty). (5) **Consistent categories** (Repertoire Actions labelled "Archer Feature", as the Paladin's) and **levels 5 to 10** with the progression pack.

(2) **Measure the four Passives** with the positions model, or a playtest note. (3) **Sharpshooter without the Hallmark choice** (the level list has it: only the Mastery and the Bane rule). (4) **One grounded out-of-combat Feature** (roleplay proposal 11: tracking, an eye for ground; better odds, no certainty). - [ ] **CHAPTER 12 (PALADIN) REVIEW: CANDIDATES (Lucas, 2026-11-07: logged to review later; nothing applied; card changes go card by card with Lucas, book/cards/README.md).** From the 21 cards, the ruler's ledger (rclasses.md section 7, 1 AP = 5.8 DE) and a comparison with PF2e's Champion, 5e's Paladin and Draw Steel's Censor. **Kept as good:** Smite once a round and scaling by AP (5e's "smite on every hit = nova" avoided; ledger 1.40 / 1.07 / 0.91); protection through Reactions (Shield of Faith reaches 5 squares, where PF2e's Champion reaction is criticized for 15 feet; Rebuke punishes attacks on allies: 4e's defend-and-punish); two real paths through the Hallmark (Smite or Lay On Hands, which spends the target's Rally Points); Kindled Wrath fits the fantasy; most Passives on budget (Radiant Zeal, Divine Surge, Searing Blows, Mending Light, Reckoning) and Final Verdict on target; light as a weapon against Hidden enemies. **Neutral:** Lay On Hands 1 AP over (1.51) and 3 AP a little over; Rebuke under (0.49) but its Pushes and Frightened are not fully modeled (a floor); levels written to 4. **Candidates:** (1) **A ledger pass, card by card with Lucas:** Divine Intervention far over (6.31: a Down prevented, 97% of days): intended as the daily miracle, or smaller (the Guard only, or no reset to 1 below the Pool); lift Font of Mercy (0.23 vs rung 1's 0.4-0.6) and Hallowed Stance (0.58 vs rung 3's 0.8-1); check Holy Wrath (1.23 / 1.08, under the Encounter band), Bulwark of Faith (1.19) and Beacon (1.03) with the positions model first (their steps and Slides aren't seen by the open field). (2) **Write the two dependencies first** (or reword the cards until they exist): Divine Power needs the Profane Tag (the Tags list: Chapter 7 candidate); Smite's "1 Bane when they try to hide" needs the Hide rules (Chapters 3 and 6 candidates). (3) **One grounded out-of-combat Feature** (roleplay proposal 11; no certainty, nothing that solves a scene: for example an oath that people of the faith recognize and trust). (4) **Consistent categories:** Repertoire Actions labelled by their list ("Paladin Encounter Action") instead of "Paladin Feature", which also names the real Features (Divine Power, Kindled Wrath); book and Foundry. (5) **Levels 5 to 10** with the progression pack.
- [ ] **CHAPTER 11 (SPECIES) REVIEW: PROPOSALS (Lucas, 2026-11-07: logged to review later; nothing applied; goes with the open "rename Species?" item and the Bestiary's reserve for Folk Traits).** From a comparison with Draw Steel's ancestries (a signature trait plus ancestry points), PF2e's ancestry feats and heritages, and D&D 2024 moving ability increases to backgrounds. **Kept as good:** equal budgets (every species nets +2 Attributes and +5 Health; the differences are trade-offs: Elf Speed 6, Dwarf Speed 4 with Steadiness 1); the Human's free +1s and the choice of threshold for the Health bonus; the plan (mostly out-of-combat things, Species Talents, a reserve of about a Talent's worth in the Bestiary: each PC's damage x 1.07). **Neutral:** Dwarf and Elf have fixed Attributes with a -1 (this stops mattering for combat if the "Modifiers as class values" candidate is adopted: the two support each other); Speed measured at 0 DE in combat (+1 Speed), so it is travel, Charge (Speed 3) and the 2 AP Crawl (Speed 5: the Dwarf can't); everyone is Medium with Reach 1. **Proposals:** (1) **An identity for each species, the light way:** one grounded signature trait (mostly out of combat: a Dwarf's stone-sense for unsafe tunnels, an Elf's long memory), a choice of 1 of 3 traits at level 1, Species Talents later; the budget is the Bestiary's reserve; the roleplay writing rules apply (no certainty, nothing that solves a scene). (2) **Write the species budget into the chapter** (+2 net Attributes, +5 Health, paired trade-offs such as Speed 6 with Steadiness 0, Speed 4 with Steadiness 1). (3) **Attribute flexibility:** keep the fixed Attributes if the Modifier candidate is adopted; otherwise an optional rule to move the species' +1s. (4) **Decide the name before writing the traits** (Species, Folk or Kin; the ruler already says "Folk Traits"). (5) **For a future Small or Large species,** check Reach, weapon Range, Squeezing and Load (Size-based Load could be a rule).
- [ ] **CHAPTER 9 (GLOSSARY) REVIEW: CANDIDATES (Lucas, 2026-11-07: logged to review later; nothing applied).** 187 rows checked by a script (order, duplicates, numbers); compared with the "glossary index" (13th Age, Draw Steel) and PF2e's index feedback. **Kept as good:** one line per term linked to its section (the build checks the links); the whole vocabulary covered; rows say what a thing is, not the whole rule. **Neutral:** the notation rows ([Mod], [W], [V] / [R], [Trait], 0 AP) come before A-Z; some rows carry numbers (Elite about 118 Health, Standard about 56, the Modifier formula, Boons "1d3-1"). **Candidates:** (1) **Sort it, and let the build enforce it** (a build_rules.py check that fails on rows out of order; the notation rows in their own small table or allowed first). Out of order today: a block of about ten rows between "Bloodied, Strained" and "Basic attack" (Barehanded, Class Actions, Dazed, Frightened, Grabbed, Hindered, Immobilized, Immunity, Pick, Repertoire); "Hazardous Terrain [HT]" before "Hazardous Terrain"; "Origin" before "Opportunity Attack"; "Resolve, Vigor" before "Regrip"; "Slowed" before "Sleep". (2) **One "Turn" row with both meanings** (the time one creature acts; the Frequency), or rename the Frequency (with Chapter 2's "Round -> Cycle" candidate). (3) **Fewer numbers in rows** (qualitative definitions, the link gives the numbers), or a build check of a kept number against its source (the Bestiary's tables, the formula). (4) **Reuse the labels** of the trait and condition candidates ("rules" / "keyword only", "helpful" / "harmful", "value"). (5) **Add the new terms in the same pack** when the economy and roleplay designs apply (Bind, Belt, Usage, Supplies, Rough Day, Load, Purse; Backgrounds, Routine...). Drift watch: Boons "1d3-1", the Modifier formula, Exhausted, Bleed's pool and Stacking all change if their candidates are adopted.
- [ ] **CHAPTER 8 REVIEW: CANDIDATES (Lucas, 2026-11-07: logged to review later; nothing applied).** From a comparison with PF2e's conditions (their tracking load), 4e's "save ends", Draw Steel's short list and the "losing your turn" complaint. **Kept as good:** one rule up front (never the same condition twice: the longer duration, the higher value; no duration = the encounter); no condition takes a whole turn (action denial split into Dazed now, Slowed next turn, Staggered without losing the Reaction AP; no Stun; Imperiled ends at the start of your turn); conditions built from others (Grabbed = Immobilized + only the holder moves you; Blinded = Darkness for your Sight); Frightened by line of sight; Empowered / Enfeebled netted per die; Taunted, Prone, Hindered, Vulnerable in a line or two. **Neutral:** about 19 conditions, nine with a value (tokens at a physical table); helpful and harmful in one list; not there on purpose or for later: charm / domination, Deafened / Silenced (D4); invisibility via Senses, poison via Sickened. **Candidates:** (1) **Simplify Exhausted** (three rules at once, and the game's only flat roll penalty): keep the Speed reduction, turn the roll penalty into Banes equal to the value (or Doomed from Exhausted 3), replace the Resolve reduction and its 12-hour Mind Save cycle with something simpler (at Exhausted 3 no Wound removal at a Rest, or at Exhausted 4 Unconscious until a Sleep); it now matters to Supplies and Rough Days. (2) **A clearer Bleed notation:** "Bleed 1d6 [V]", the end written apart ("Ends: Body or Instinct Save, DC 15"); always write the pool (no hidden "the Attributes set the pool" default). (3) **Immobilized's DC rules in a short sidebar** ("Moving an Immobilized creature"), so the condition is two lines. (4) **Imperiled: Vulnerable 2** (or keep 3 as a named source with its limit, if the Circumstance candidate is adopted). (5) **Labels instead of repeated text:** a "value" tag and a "helpful / harmful" tag on each name, explained once in the intro ("always has a value" is repeated in nine entries).
- [ ] **CHAPTER 7 REVIEW: CANDIDATES (Lucas, 2026-11-07: logged to review later; nothing applied; goes with the open "TRAITS: reorganize" item).** From a comparison with PF2e's traits, 4e's keywords and Draw Steel's ability keywords; the cards counted (Strike 51, Weapon 47, Melee 25, Ranged 19, Shot 17, Divine 17, Self 16, Area 10, Focus 10, Handle 6, Power 6, Move 5, Body 5, Save 4, Gun 2, Instinct 2, Signature 2, Appeal 1, Fear 1; Nonlethal, Split and Mind have entries but no card). **Kept as good:** a small set (about 20) covering every card; Weapon Actions take Melee / Ranged / Shot / Gun from the weapon (one card for every weapon); Split and Signature; Attribute traits (roll the highest, "Vs. the one used to roll"); Area ignores Senses; creature Tags kept apart from traits (as Draw Steel's monster keywords). **Neutral:** the 3-Strike limit here and its "other creatures' turns" rule in Chapter 2; Handle and the free Release. **Candidates:** (1) **Organize the chapter by the trait groups** (Roll, Requires, Kind, Damage, Keywords: build_rules.py GROUPS) and **mark each entry "rules" or "keyword only"** (a small label in the book and in Foundry's tooltips): PF2e's most-cited complaint is not the number of traits but that you can't tell which carry rules (its players ask for rule traits highlighted and passive tags in a separate list). (2) **A Divine entry** (17 cards, none today; keyword only: the Paladin's features and Profane Tags refer to it). (3) **Decide Focus** (10 cards; "some features affect concentration", none does yet): a small rule (one Focus effect at a time, or it ends if you are Dazed or go Down) or keyword only, reserved. (4) **One source for movement rules:** the Move trait's movement rules (no ending in a creature's square; an unwilling creature's squares are Difficult Terrain) move to Chapter 3, with a link. (5) **Text:** the Weapon entry's "a Weapon roll normally targets Armor" (the Vs. line says it); the entries are ### directly under the chapter's # (no ## level); write the creature Tags list (Undead, Beast, Profane...) when the Bestiary needs it.
- [ ] **CHAPTER 6 REVIEW: CANDIDATES (Lucas, 2026-11-07: logged to review later; nothing applied).** From a comparison with PF2e's basic actions and hand economy, its Aid, and Draw Steel's maneuvers (Aid Attack, Knockback, Grab, Hide). **Kept as good:** a short list (18) with one broad catch-all (Interact, "broader than most"); the Handle ladder (Shove, Relocate, Haul at 1 / 2 / 3 AP, one pattern); Charge, Steel Yourself, Stand, Crawl, Leaning; Reload as a roll an ally can make; Opportunity Attack as everyone's Reaction; one layout and a Flavor line on every card. **Neutral:** the hand economy (Interact, Regrip, Release; softened by Expert Mastery and Foundry); Relocate is At-will while Shove, Trip and Haul are Round; Opportunity Attack's "within 3 squares" vs threatening by the weapon's range. **Candidates:** (1) **Add the missing Basic Actions:** Hide and Search (with the Hide rules: Chapter 3's gap; Senses, Hearing, Surprise and Darkness need them); Grab, and print Break Free (it exists with "book": false while Immobilized and Grabbed refer to escaping); **Aid (1 AP):** an ally's next roll against an enemy within your Reach gains 1 Boon, until the end of your next turn (cheap, unlike PF2e's action + reaction + DC 20 for +1; Draw Steel's Aid Attack is the model); **Use an Item:** AP = the item's Usage, the Belt -1 (items-economy.md). (2) **Price the Handle Actions with the ruler** (1 AP = 5.8 DE; Prone about 2.3 DE, Pushed 1 about 1.1: Trip 1 AP works only on a Tier 3, about 0.7 DE; Trip 2 AP about 3 DE for 11.6; Shove 1 AP under 1 DE): strengthen the low Versions (Trip 1 AP: Prone on Tier 2 and 3) or keep them situational and say so on the card ("best against a wall, a ledge or a hazard"). (3) **Consistent frequencies:** Relocate at Round like its siblings, or a line saying why it is At-will. (4) **An overview table** at the start of the chapter, by purpose (moving, attacking, handling, recovering), with the AP. (5) **A sidebar on why there is no Ready or Delay** (unspent AP become Guard; Reactions carry each class's identity).
- [ ] **CHAPTER 5 REVIEW: CANDIDATES (Lucas, 2026-11-07: logged to review later; nothing applied).** From a comparison with PF2e's "+level to everything", 5e's odd ability scores, and character creation in PF2e and Draw Steel. **Kept as good:** "Levels give options; Stages change the numbers" (bounded math, Modifiers never above +4; PF2e's level-as-the-main-stat avoided); Distinct Choices (against min-maxing); Attributes don't touch Health; one shape for every class at level 1; a Talent every level, Stage Talents at 5 and 8. **Neutral:** Checks use the raw Attribute while Modifiers were compressed; the permanent Health assignment (softened by the one-third floor); the Signature Modifier as a third name. **Candidates:** (1) **Weapon and Power Modifiers as class values, not Attributes (Lucas's idea):** the class gives two values (the Paladin +2 and +1, for example) and the player chooses which is the Weapon Modifier and which the Power Modifier; Stages raise them, possibly unevenly (a suggested track, cap +4: level 1 +2 / +1; Stage 2 +1 to either, +3 / +1 or +2 / +2; Stage 3 +1 to both, +4 / +2 or +3 / +3); a class may differ (a pure martial +2 / +0, a caster favoring Power, a fixed order if its fantasy needs it); Signature stays the higher of the two. Why: the current formula (Attribute / 2 + 1) is already a fixed track in disguise (every R build starts at +2 / +2; an Attribute is at most 2 at level 1) with traps (even values add nothing, where the Stage +1 lands, two Modifiers wanting one Attribute); the change removes the dead points, takes all combat pressure off the Attributes (no dumping; "no Attribute is king" in full), gives a real level 1 identity (sword-first or faith-first Paladin), deletes the ÷ 2 + 1 formula, the "both want the same Attribute" paragraph and the Modifier half of Distinct Choices, and matches the ruler's baseline (+2 / +3 / +4). Precedent: Lancer's Grit (one attack bonus by level, whatever the stats). Watch: say in one place what Attributes still do (Checks, Saves, Defenses vs an Attribute, Initiative, Wound rolls, the economy jobs, skills, the Attribute Actions such as Shove and Trip); the secondary Modifier is lower than today (+1 vs +2), so a weapon-first PC has 1 less Ward Defense and weaker Power Actions: run the ruler on the +2 / +1 split first; fiction (training, not muscle; Attribute Actions keep raw strength meaningful). To rewrite: Chapter 5, Chapter 1's "Boon on Body Rolls" table (simpler), the glossary, the Paladin and Archer stat blocks, the Bestiary's NPC Weapon / Power Attribute, Foundry's builder, the ruler's builds. Replaces the dead-points options of this review. (2) **Quick Build:** each class marks one default for every level 1 choice (Draw Steel's gold icon) and the book has one or two pregenerated characters per class (about 10 decisions at level 1 today). (3) **Complete "Building a Character":** gear and starting money (100 SC, items-economy.md), placeholders for skills and Backgrounds (roleplay-skills.md).
- [ ] **CHAPTER 4 REVIEW: PROPOSALS (Lucas, 2026-11-07: logged to review later; nothing applied).** From a comparison with 4e's healing surges, 5e's dying and pop-up healing, single vs separate mental-damage tracks (Fate, Daggerheart) and lingering-injury tables. **Kept as good:** one pool with two thresholds (one track, and mental harm counts as much as physical; the one-third floor); Rally Points as 4e's healing surges (only a safe Rest refills them, tied to Supplies and Rough Days); keep fighting while Dying or Shattered (no 5e "whack-a-mole" sitting out); Wounds that scale (+1d6 a Wound) and heal at a Rest; Bloodied / Strained as pure hooks; Guard, instances, Nonlethal and NPCs going Down. **Neutral:** back up as soon as damage drops below the Pool (pop-up healing softened: no Steel Yourself while Down, the table's Wound stays); Wound rolls use Body and Mind only (fine if Appeal and Instinct have jobs elsewhere); strong attrition (no healing on a Sleep); Nonlethal fights are cheap (a Breather removes it). **Proposals:** (1) **The Unconscious healing:** say that Sleep isn't the Unconscious condition (else 8 hours of Sleep heal 8 Rally Points' worth); the hourly healing only brings you back below your Health Pool (enough to wake), and not from damage you chose to take (a friend knocking you out). (2) **One shared Down procedure:** one table for Dying and Shattered with columns (collapse or give up / keep fighting each turn / on damage), each state naming its Check and its table; the "Tier 2 and 3: no effect" rows become "on a Tier 1, roll on the table". (3) **The sheet tracks 3 numbers:** Vigor damage, Resolve damage, and how much of it is Nonlethal (a sheet and Foundry change). (4) **A cost for getting back up (to test):** a creature that rises from Down is Slowed 1 on its next turn (or keep pop-up healing as it is). (5) **Write the Wound tables:** recoverable severities (most Wounds go at a Rest, permanent ones rare and marked), some player choice (choose between two at "moderate"), no repeated bands.
- [ ] **CHAPTER 3 REVIEW: CANDIDATES (Lucas, 2026-11-07: logged to review later; nothing applied).** From a comparison with 4e's push / pull / slide and diagonals, Draw Steel's forced movement and stability, PF2e's detection rules and grid cover in 5e. **Kept as good:** Forced Movement (close to Draw Steel's: 1 damage a square to each creature in a collision, stability = Steadiness; better: the origin aims, walls 2 a square, Reposition apart, the four-movement table); the "Move Action / you moved / you left your square" table; Senses on one scale (Clear, Obscured, Hidden, Unsensed; PF2e's two axes are widely called confusing); Light reusing the area shapes, the brightest wins, Dark Sight rare (5e's "everyone has darkvision" avoided); Cover as blocked corners from the center of your square (one origin, one number; fits the Circumstance candidate); Deadly Hazards kill only Minor enemies (others are Imperiled); Hazardous Terrain patterns and the turn-cycle limit; Size as a rank, its simplification stated. **Neutral:** diagonals 1-2-1 but Forced Movement and Reposition count 1 (two counts in one game); movement counted across the whole turn; the Pushed / Pulled cone edges and the straight-line ties; the Line's long rules. **Candidates:** (1) **Simpler Forced Movement:** Pushed = each square farther from the origin than the last, Pulled = each square closer, no cones, **diagonals 1-2-1 like everything else** (one count for the whole game); check with the ruler's positions model (positions.mjs) that pushes don't get stronger. (2) **Squeezing:** a fixed penalty instead of "Vulnerable equal to half their level" (the only level-scaled number in the chapter; Vulnerable 5 at level 10 would be the biggest stacker, outside the Circumstance cap): Doomed alone, or Doomed and Vulnerable 1. (3) **Write the gaps, in order:** Hide and Sneak (Surprise, Senses and Hearing need them), cones, objects and Breach. (4) **A movement quick-reference page** from the existing tables (the three questions, the four movements, Cover, Senses) for physical tables.
- [ ] **CHAPTER 2 REVIEW: CANDIDATES (Lucas, 2026-11-07: logged to review later; nothing applied).** From a comparison with Draw Steel's alternating initiative, PF2e's three actions and attack penalty, 13th Age's escalation die and "unspent actions become defense" designs. **Kept as good:** the zipper by side (no alpha strikes in a row); unspent AP as Guard (defense as a choice, never "do nothing"); At-will Banes per specific Action (gentler than PF2e's -5 / -10, rewards variety); the defined Reaction moments and the movement tip; Escalation tied to the fight's story by the class hooks, carried by Momentum, only Elites among NPCs; precise Linked Actions, Requirements, Willing, Targets; durations tied to initiative slots. **Neutral:** rolled order vs players choosing (Draw Steel); three AP pools (turn, kept, Reaction); round 1 is the weakest round (by design); "per turn" counts other creatures' turns. **Candidates:** (1) **Players choose the order within their side** (optional or default): the zipper still decides when a PC slot comes up, the players choose which PC takes it; Swapping Initiative and the frozen-AP rule go; durations end at the end of the creature's turn instead of its slot; Instinct still decides which side starts (cost: Instinct's role shrinks a little; Foundry's tracker changes). (2) **Rename the Round Frequency to "Cycle"** (once per turn-cycle), so "round" means only the round number and durations. (3) **"Next" effects on others:** they last until the end of that creature's next turn (the rule today covers only "your next Action"; Rally the Tribe gives allies a "next" Boon). (4) **Text:** the Defenses table ("Actions roll against one of these", not "a creature has three kinds": Vs. DC isn't a creature's Defense); a small "Paying for a Reaction" table (what can pay on your turn and on others', and what happens to the Guard). (5) **Playtest watch:** turtling with unspent AP (the ruler prices it; check it is chosen for good reasons).
- [ ] **NAMED BOONS AND BANES, THE CIRCUMSTANCE LIMIT: CANDIDATE (Lucas, 2026-11-07; nothing applied; goes with the flat Boons candidate in "CHAPTER 1 REVIEW").** The idea (Lucas): unnamed Boons and Banes stack freely; **named** ones stack too, but only up to their own limit (name only what needs a limit: the opposite of PF1e, which names every type and keeps the highest of each). Closest systems: Savage Worlds' Gang Up (+1 per extra ally, max +4, reduced by the target's adjacent allies; Support has its own +4 cap) and 4e (untyped bonuses stack, combat advantage counts once). Today's rules already do it without the names (Flanking up to 3, Cover keeps the highest, Range 1-2, Vulnerable by value). **The candidate:** (1) **Circumstance, max 3 Banes:** Cover (1-3, the highest), Range (1-2), shooting into melee (1), a Prone target against Ranged Actions (1), Obscured (1), a Long weapon against an adjacent creature (1). **Circumstance, max 3 Boons:** Flanking (1 per other flanker), a Prone target in melee (1), Obscured (its Actions against you: 1); Cover's Boons on Saves count as Circumstance for the creature saving. **Flanking's own cap is dropped** (Lucas): the Circumstance limit of 3 does the job (this replaces the "FLANKING: CAP AT 2 BOONS" candidate if adopted). (2) **Named, outside Circumstance, each with its own limit:** **Engaged 2** (shooting while you are Engaged: outside on purpose, Lucas: it is about you and it is a choice, you can step away first; inside, an archer whose target has High cover would shoot point-blank for free), **Repeat** (At-will: 1, then 2; naturally capped by 3 Strikes a turn), **conditions** by their value (the highest: Frightened 1, Taunted 1, Sickened N, Vulnerable N), **Grip 3** (a Two-handed weapon in one hand). (3) **Unnamed** (cards, items, NPC abilities) stack, with a writing rule: **an unnamed source gives 1, rarely 2, never "1 per X"** (anything that scales gets a name and a limit). The worst case (Engaged 2 + into melee 1 + maximum range 2 + Medium cover 2, at +4 against DC 13): today 7 Banes, Tier 1 59% / Tier 3 13%; with Engaged outside the cap 5 Banes, 48% / 17% (inside it would be 3, 35% / 24%). The realistic maximum drops from 9+ to about 6, and 4+ Bane attacks stay rare (0.21% in the map model). Three words for +1 stay distinct: a **bonus** (Defenses and damage: the highest only), a **Boon** (rolls: stacks), a **named Boon** (stacks to its limit). If applied: Chapter 1 (Boons and Banes), Chapter 2 (Stacking), Chapter 3 (Range, Engaged, Flanking, Cover, Senses), the glossary, the ruler (map.mjs, calc.mjs), Foundry (the roll's breakdown in its tooltip).
- [ ] **ROLEPLAY AND SKILLS: PROPOSALS (2026-11-07; `book/roleplay-skills.md`; Lucas reviews them in depth later; nothing decided).** From research on 4e's skill challenges, PF2e's skill feats, Draw Steel's perks and negotiation, 13th Age / Daggerheart backgrounds, Burning Wheel's Beliefs, the skill-list debate and Lancer's split: six failure modes to avoid (permission, the menu, certainty, budget competition, the roll replacing play, two games) and 12 proposals (anyone can attempt anything plausible; no certainty; separate budgets; the idea sets the roll; Routine; tier guidance; 8-12 broad skills raised by Talents; Backgrounds as 1 Boon; Challenges and Negotiations for big moments with "repeat an approach: 1 Bane"; Drives and Sparks; one grounded out-of-combat Feature per class; the bridge between story and combat). Seven decisions listed at the end of the document.
- [ ] **CHAPTER 1 REVIEW: CANDIDATES (Lucas, 2026-11-07: logged to review later; nothing applied).** From a comparison with Draw Steel, PF2e, Savage Worlds, Shadow of the Demon Lord and doubles mechanics (sources in the session). **Kept as good:** 3 tiers on 2d12 (Modifiers matter: +1 is about 9% of a roll), one roll for several targets with tiers per target, Resist / Weak once per Action, explicit Riders, the rules language, the exploding 12 on the roll (not on damage). **Neutral:** weapons' Tier 1 is 0 (misses exist, unlike Draw Steel: some Actions could get a little Tier 1 if whiffs feel bad); Doubles raise 1 roll in 12 whatever the odds (a GM note: don't ask for a roll for the impossible); two paths to a Critical for a rare payoff (kept: the Escalation hook); the six Rolls and their table are dense (a one-line intro). **Candidates:** (1) **Favored's Doubles simplified:** Favored keeps the two highest of 3d12 and **any two matching faces are Doubles** (no pair comparison; the multi-target rule becomes "ignore the lowest die"); slightly stronger. **Priced (2026-11-07, exact math: calc.mjs against an enumeration of the variant; Lucas liked it, still a candidate):** Favored at +0 / +2 / +4 vs DC 13, today → any pair: Tier 1 13.9 / 6.9 / 2.8% (unchanged: today a pair already lifts any failure), Tier 3 35.8 → 38.8 / 48.6 → 52.3 / 62.4 → 66.3% (+3 to +4 points), Critical 7.6 → 8.8 / 11.0 → 12.6 / 15.2 → 17.3%; three dice show a pair 23.6% of the time. If Favored grows too strong, trim it elsewhere, not here. **Same session, should Doubles be dropped? No, keep them:** on a normal roll they are worth about +0.5 Modifier (at +2 vs DC 13 without them: Tier 1 27.8 → 31.2%, Tier 3 26.7 → 24.7%), but they give **half the Criticals** (4.5% → 2.3% without; it slows the Escalation hook) and most of Favored's edge (without them Favored fails 12.4% instead of 6.9%, Critical 11.0% → 4.7%); their only real cost is the pair comparison, which this candidate removes. Precedents: Daggerheart (2d12, matching dice = a Critical Success even below the DC), Draw Steel (2d10 tiers, a natural 19-20 = Tier 3 and an extra main action). If applied: 01-roll.md (Favored and Doomed: the text, its example, procedure step 4; Several Targets), the ruler (calc.mjs tierProbs, Favored branch; re-run spec.mjs), Foundry (rolls.keepDice, the dice tests such as t42). (2) **Flat Boons and Banes** (+1 / -1; the 2026-11-06 numbers: the tiers move 1 point at most; a feel choice), with the cap of 3 if wanted. (3) **Save cards labelled from the target's side** ("Fails / Partial / Resists" next to Tier 1 / 2 / 3; presentation only, book and Foundry). (4) **Text:** the Summary gets the Critical and "a Doomed roll has no Doubles"; a one-line intro over the Rolls table; the GM note on the impossible.
- [ ] **ITEMS AND ECONOMY: DESIGNED WITH LUCAS, LOGGED PART BY PART (2026-11-06; `book/items-economy.md`; nothing in rules-src yet).** Part 1 Items logged: no mandatory items, no "too cheap not to buy" items (the Tailwind problem), no Attribute is king; the Bestiary never assumes items (no item touches Modifiers, Defenses, Health or Rally Points; a narrow +1 is the exception); the Bind trait (Stage + 1 bound); consumables with a Usage entry, the Belt (3, -1 Usage, filled on a Breather); prices (100 SC per DE a fight, 5 SC per DE of a use, sell at 1/2); healing potions 5 / 10 / 15, one per Sleep; Upkeep per Sleep, Dulled until Repaired; no item-level gate (market level, Upkeep, slots); weapons and armor in one price band, enchantments that move (10% / 25%); unidentified items, Exalted weapons, crafting and a price list for later. Part 2 Actions Out of Combat logged: attempting a hostile Action against an aware creature rolls Initiative first; Surprised = every enemy acting against you is Hidden or Unsensed (Talents and niche GM leeway; the surprise round is still in the works); out of combat, effects in turns, rounds or until the end of the encounter don't work and "during the encounter" abilities can't be used; conditions follow how they end; every "next" effect gets EoT; Encounter Actions used between fights stay spent; Round and Turn Actions allowed out of combat; long-duration effects never grant combat bonuses; the bag-of-rats note; a preparation bonus (+1 Escalation) only when you know what you will face. Part 3 Loot and Money logged: CC / SC / GC (silver main); treasure per level (150 to 1200 SC, start 100) from enemy loot, found items and rewards (30 / 30 / 40); lots by role (Minion 1/2 to Elite 4) worth the monster's own level's treasure / 100; five types (Valuables, Arcana, Idols, Hides, Salvage) with weights 1 / 1 / 2 / 3 / 5; towns with multipliers, a market level and frontier prices; travelling merchants; optional quick-sell and rare drops; haggling as a Designer's Note and Talents. Part 4 Carrying and Expenses logged: the pack as a grid of squares, the paper doll (1 armor, up to 4 weapons and shields on the body counting the hands), Load = 10 + 2 x Body, over Load Speed -2, a mule; the Kit (no weight, wears down on Rough Days); Supplies prepaid per PC, 1 SC a day, spent on each Sleep (7 weigh nothing); Forage in the wild only, Work in town; Rough Days (-1 Rally Point at the next Rest each, Exhausted 1 from the 3rd in a row); a shared purse as a table option; one economy job per Attribute; base building and Retreat later. **All four parts logged; the open list is at the end of the document.**

- [x] **THE CONDITIONS PASS (2026-11-04, pack R-classes, part 2; decided with Lucas in the brainstorm after v0.0.235):** the vocabulary the class cards were missing. **Chapter 8:** **Bleed X (Attributes)** (weapon-trees.md's decided rule, moved into the book: damage at the end of each of your turns, Vigor for Bo/In and Resolve for Mi/Ap unless the pool is written, Interact (1 AP) and a Save against DC 13 to end it, healing doesn't, the higher average kept), **Blinded** (you can't see: heard creatures are Hidden, sight Rolls Doomed), **Dazed N** (you lose N AP right away, never the Reaction AP; the rest from your next turn's AP, then it ends: the "lose AP now" condition, Slowed is the next-turn one), **Frightened** (of a source: 1 Bane on all your Rolls, the Save that ends it included, and you can't willingly move closer, while you see the source: cutting Line of Effect stops it), **Grabbed** (held: Immobilized, only the holder moves you; ends when it lets go, leaves your Reach, goes Down, or you Break Free), **Sickened N** (N Banes on Body and Instinct Saves, nothing swallowed: no potions), **Staggered** (no Reactions; the Reaction AP waits), **Taunted** (by a creature: 1 Bane against anyone else); the chapter's intro says the effect sets the duration (no duration = the encounter). **Chapter 1:** **Resist N / Weak N / Immunity (subtype)** per creature (a Hatchling Resist 2 Fire, an Ancient dragon Immunity): applied **once per Action or effect to all its damage of that subtype, both pools together** (never per instance: Lucas, PF2's trap), the resisting creature chooses the part it reduces first, the origin where Weak's extra goes; Immunity is 0; a Bleed counts each time it lands. **Riders** (new rule): what follows the damage in a tier cell happens only if that damage is dealt (more than 0 after Resist and Immunity); Guard doesn't stop a rider (Chapter 4 says so too); an effect that must happen regardless goes on the Effects row or in a cell of its own; the designer decides by placement, no "if it deals damage" on cards. **Chapter 2:** "What a Reaction Responds To": the list of moments cards can use (a creature would leave a square or Stands, would enter a square adjacent to you or an ally, ends a Move Action, would be Force Moved or fall Prone, makes a Strike or would damage someone, would use a Power Action, scores a Tier 3 or a Critical, becomes Bloodied or Strained, goes Down, uses a Rally Point, Charges, Flanks). **Bestiary:** the step table rows (Mild: Taunted, Sickened 1, Bleed 1-2, Staggered on a creature with only Opportunity Attack; Moderate: Dazed 1, Grabbed, Bleed 1d4-1d6, Frightened of a creature it can reach, Staggered on a creature with a real Reaction; Severe: Blinded, Frightened of a creature it must approach; 4 steps: Dazed 2, Bleed 2d6), with the arithmetic behind them (a Bane is about 10% of an attack, Doomed about half; Dazed 1 a quarter of a Standard's turn). **Glossary:** the new terms, and Hindered and Immobilized, which had no row. **The ruler** learned Dazed (AP from the next turn), Staggered, Blinded (Doomed Strikes), Frightened (its Bane), Taunted and Bleed on NPCs and PCs (map.mjs applyCondition, spec.mjs), and positions.mjs prices them (prices.md section 5). **Decided along the way (Lucas):** NPCs get Reactions of their own in R-bestiary (Reaction Specials), so Staggered's worth against them is their Reaction's, not Opportunity Attack's; no PC-only conditions, Staggered on PCs is just rare; Marked is not a condition (Marked Quarry stays the Archer's term, Challenge's effect becomes Taunted); Hide stays its own session (B2); Resist / Weak numbers are per creature, not per Stage. **Not written:** Burning (Bleed with a subtype covers it), Deafened / Silenced (D4), Rooted (Immobilized covers it). **Foundry:** the new conditions need effect items and, for Dazed, Bleed and Staggered, a mechanic: R-Foundry (foundry-backlog.md). **Next: the Paladin's cards rewritten with the vocabulary** (the proposal in one message, answered by exception), then the Archer's.

Apex: **Deathmark** (2 AP, a Shot at Holy Wrath 2's dice, 1 Boon against your Quarry; if the target goes Down, a new Quarry and a free Weapon Attack against it) and **Open Season** (2 AP: a Weapon Attack against your Quarry, then until the end of your next turn it is Vulnerable 2 and your allies' Strikes against it deal an extra [V] 1d6 Pi).
  - **The fights at the sheet's fit** (rclasses.md section 2; a Smite and a Lay On Hands Paladin, a Double Shot and a Rolling Shot Archer): 4 Standards 3.7 rounds, **7% of the party's Health, a Down in 9%**; 6 Standards 5.5 r · 29% · Down 40% · won 86%; 2 Elites + 2 Standards 5.6 r · 14% · Down 21% · won 97%. The rebuilt PCs are safer than the sheet's quarter target (Shield of Faith and the ladder): **R-bestiary re-fits the NPCs to them** (section 12.3: PCs first, NPCs after).
  - **Talents:** Whirlwind and Cut Through stay as they are (item 21 asked for a re-cost at 5.8 DE an AP: Whirlwind's 2 AP Encounter is 1.6 × the band's floor, inside it; Cut Through's 2 AP Round is at the Round band's bottom with its Evade 2 counted). The Opportunity Attack Talent was already gone (R-core).

- [ ] **PACK R, THE CORE DECISIONS (Lucas, 2026-11-04, brainstorm after the numbers; nothing applied; the full list with its numbers: `book/rework-r.md` section 12; what the sheet still proposes: section 13).** (1) 4 AP + 1 Reaction AP; **unspent turn AP become Guard, flat 2 / 3 / 4 by Stage per AP, adding**, until your next turn; the Reaction AP never converts. (2) A Reaction is worth at least 1 AP; a basic Reaction for everyone; Hallmark Reactions budgeted. (3) A normal fight at least 75% in the players' favor; the Specials go into the calibration; PCs first, NPCs re-fitted after. (4) **Health:** the class gives a high and a low value, the player assigns them; the species adds a flat bonus on its side (Human chooses); **Attributes out of Health**; growth by Stage; 30 at level 1 → 60-70 at level 10; the 65 / 35 norm; a floor of a third. Supersedes LV's ×1.5 and the (Level + 17) / 16 factor. (5) **Vigor and Resolve** are the thresholds, **Vigor damage [V] and Resolve damage [R]** the pools (renamed in R-core; parsers read both spellings meanwhile). (6) **Stage is the number clock:** one Attribute +1 at each Stage, Modifiers +1 at Stage 3, never above +4, Checks use the raw Attribute (supersedes the +1 at 5 / +2 at 8 step); Guard, Overcast, Momentum and Weapon Mastery on Stages. (7) No bloat: flat accuracy and DCs, Health ×2 at most, dice capped. (8) **Escalation:** a level; the whole ladder at level 1; the **Apex** (the Escalation 3 Encounter ability, a level 1 pick); every fight starts at 1 (Momentum 2); +1 on a Critical once an encounter; one hook per class (Paladin: an ally within 5 becomes Bloodied or goes Down; Archer: the Marked Quarry goes Down); Elites +1 a round from round 2; no meta currency (a GM sidebar instead). (9) Level 1 = Hallmark Action, Hallmark Reaction, an Encounter Pick, three Passives, the Apex; a Talent every level; asymmetric Picks; the classes restarted from the Paladin. (10) Rally Points = class Rally + Stage. (11) Enemy Bloodied / Strained hooks are "when it becomes"; "while" riders on your own Actions are fine; Overcast as the ladder; Slowed lowers the 4; Minions 3 AP; some NPCs attack WD. **Next: the decision sheet (pack R prep part 2) with the ruler modeling the Specials, the Health line, the Stage Modifiers, Guard, the hooks and the two dice-cut shapes; then R-core, R-classes (Paladin, Archer), R-bestiary, R-Foundry ×2, playtest 4** (rework-r.md, end of section 13).

Level list = the charter's template (L1: Sharpshooter + Marked Quarry, the Signature **Double Shot or Rolling Shot**, 1 Talent, Killer's Focus + Pinning Shot; L2: Encounter Marksman's Shot, 1 Talent; L3: Daily Hunter's Focus, Passive **Elusive** (new: the first [Strike] Action against you each round takes 1 Bane, Escalation 1)). **Sharpshooter** gives the Signature choice, carries Bow Mastery and removes the into-melee Bane for [Shot] Actions; **Marked Quarry** gets the decided floor ([B] [Mod] on a Tier 1); **Double Shot 3 AP** = Evade 1, then as 2 AP (the Boons gone); **Rolling Shot** 1 / 2 / 3 AP: Evade 1 / 2 / 3 + a Weapon Attack, +1d6 / +2d6 at 2 / 3 AP; **Marksman's Shot** reworked (1[W] + 1d8 / + [Mod] + 3d6 / + [Mod] + 4d6, Vulnerable 2 on Tier 3); **Precise Aim leaves the class** (the Talent Aim). **Numbers:** sustained 102-104% at levels 1-2 (today 96%), real Nova 117-128%, Health 86%, the Standard day 79% → 87-88% cleared, shots into melee 29 a day → 0; level 3 sustained 109-110% (Momentum 2 at level 3 and Hunter's Focus: question 5). **Found:** the P1 Paladin's Radiant Zeal (+11% sustained at level 3) is against model A (Options step).
- [ ] **LV, THE PROGRESSION PREP (pack LV, 2026-10-29; book/ruler only, nothing in rules-src, no Foundry version): DECIDED; superseded on 2026-11-04 (pack R core decisions) in two points: Health ×1.5 at level 10 and the (Level + 17) / 16 factor give way to the Stage Health line (30 → 60-70), and the Modifier step +1 / +2 to Attributes +1 a Stage with Modifiers +1 at Stage 3, cap +4.** **Lucas decided (2026-10-29): all five defaults** (model A; hard CV 1¼ and deadly 1½ a PC; Spell Mastery at level 8 with 2[W], keep the best and the round-1 floor as Magic Talents; CV by level gap by tier ×1 / ×0.8 / ×0.65; Health factor (Level + 17) / 16). **To apply:** the CV scale, level gap and NPC Health/damage by level with E3 (Bestiary); the Health factor and Modifier steps in the Core Rules with E3 (PCs and NPCs together), then Foundry; 2[W] and Spell Mastery with D2; keep the best, Wild Magic, the floor with D4. Was: (`book/progression-lv.md`; the tables: `book/ruler/progression.md`, `node book/ruler/progression.mjs`). Three growth models run through the ruler at levels 1-10 with a stand-in weapon user and caster (same level 1 budget): **A Bounded** (Modifier +3 at level 5, +4 at 8, Mastery 2[W] and keep the best at 8; fight damage +17% at level 5, +61% at 8-10), **B Escalation-heavy** (+33% / +90%), **C +10% a level** (+46% / +102%). **Findings:** (1) fight length holds in every model when NPC Health follows the PCs' damage (easy 2.5, standard 3.5 rounds; 19% / 33% of the party's Health); NPC Health ×1.22 at level 1 for 3.5-round fights (against the stand-ins), then A ×1.17 at level 5, ×1.63 at level 10. (2) **Today's CV scale misses the hard and deadly lengths:** CV 1.25 a PC lasts 4.0-4.2 rounds (with a Daily), CV 1.5 lasts 5.4-6 (61-74% Health), CV 2 is a wipe; Elites count for more than CV 2 (E3). (3) **The level gap** is √(Health × damage) of the lower NPC against one of the party's level: about ×0.9 a level (not ×0.8), by tier in model A (×1 / ×0.8 / ×0.65). (4) **Weapon user against caster:** the caster's round 1 is 94-112% of the weapon user's without a floor (the weapon user holds Escalation, the caster spends it); the floor adds 2-5%; the Overcast limit only shapes round 1; **keep the best (+10%) can't match Mastery 2[W] (+31%)**: Spell Mastery (+1 Power die) does (103%). (5) Rally Points grow with level, so days get easier. **Defaults proposed (section 5 of the document):** model A; difficulty by CV a PC easy 3/4, standard 1, hard 1 1/4, deadly 1 1/2; Spell Mastery with 2[W] at level 8, keep the best and the round-1 floor as Magic Talents; CV by level gap by tier; Health factor (Level + 17) / 16. **For A1:** no sustained growth inside tier 1 (levels 1-3); this answers E2's open question about the +10% curve.
- [ ] **PROGRESSION AND MAGIC: DECIDED IN THE BRAINSTORM (Lucas, 2026-10-29; nothing in rules-src yet; the numbers come from the progression prep pack, LV, before A1).** (1) **Level cap 10**, in tiers 1-4, 5-7, 8-10 (open to 15 later). (2) **Fight length at every level:** easy 2.5 rounds, standard 3.5, hard 4-4.5, deadly 5. (3) **No number bloat:** Modifiers at most +4 (+2 → +3 → +4 over the levels); Defenses only from armor; NPC accuracy stays flat, so a lower-level NPC still deals some damage. (4) **CV changes with the level gap** between an NPC and the party (the factor measured by the ruler; about ×0.8 per level below): CV balances encounters at every level. (5) **Health grows slowly** (about ×1.5 at level 10, not ×2: the level factor to be set by LV); NPC Health only as much as keeps the fight lengths. (6) **Weapon growth: Mastery raises 1[W] to 2[W]** at a high step (D2). (7) **Spells are Power Actions with the [Spell] trait**: named spells (Fireball, Magic Missile), on lists, open to anyone through Talents, mostly Power Modifier; classes differ by repertoire (a Wizard knows many and readies few; a Sorcerer knows few, Overcasts more easily). No [P] die and no new casting mechanic: Power damage is dice + effects (bigger variance than Weapon). (8) **Overcast:** each spell card has an Overcast line, paid with **Escalation only**; the most you can Overcast rises by tier (1 / 2 / 3). (9) **The Escalation cap stays 3**; where a fight starts rises by tier (Momentum up to 2, up to 3 at levels 8-10), plus features that build it faster. (10) **Anyone can Overcast the card's own option**; Magic Talents add options and limits; magic-first classes start with Magic Talents (as classes can start with Mastery). (11) **Talent categories as traits:** Combat, Mobility, Magic, Skill; **theme trees keyed on traits** (Fire Adept → Pyromancer → Wildfire works for every [Fire] spell), chains of 2-3, not a tree per spell. (12) **Keep the best** for Power damage (from a level: roll one extra die, drop the lowest); die steps come from Overcast and Talents; **exploding dice as a Magic Talent** (Wild Magic). (13) **Spells must not feel bad:** Overcast is the caster's Escalation Active (weapon users spend Escalation on theirs); a spell with 0 Overcast is a complete Action on its band; free growth matched (Mastery 2[W] against keep the best); a round-1 floor for magic-first classes ("once per fight, Overcast 1 without Escalation"; a Magic Talent for others); casters get few "hold Escalation" passives. Ruler check: damage by round 1-4 and per fight, weapon build against caster build, levels 1 / 5 / 10: the fight totals equal, the caster's round 1 at least 90% of the weapon user's. **Also decided:** Talents are left out of the class builds (classes compared with classes); the charter's +10% a level curve is replaced by this.
- [ ] **E2, POSITIONS IN THE RULER (pack E2, 2026-10-29; book/ruler only, no rule changed, no Foundry version).** The fights now happen on a grid (`book/ruler/map.mjs`; `map on` in fights.txt) and `book/ruler/positions.mjs` prices movement and conditions (prices.md section 5; ruler/README.md "Positions"). **Findings for Lucas (level 1):** (1) **positions cost the Archer a fifth of its damage**: real sustained 121% → 96% of the baseline (budget 100-105%), the Paladin 87% → 81%; the Archer's shots carry 1.6 Banes from the map (72% into melee, 53% with Cover, mostly a creature in the line; the Engaged Banes cost nothing because it steps away). **For A1:** the Archer's rewrite starts from 96%, and the into-melee Bane is its biggest cost (an Archer Feature or Talent could remove it, as Steady Nerves removes the Engaged Banes, which a kiting Archer never takes). (2) **Hard fights get much harder on the map** (normal fights don't): the P1 Smite party's Standard day cleared 95% → 79%, Downs a day 0.8 → 2.3, the 2 Elites + 2 Standards fight 45% → 60% of the party's Health: fights last longer and NPCs focus the hurt PCs. **For E3:** refit the Bestiary on the map. (3) **Condition prices** on an NPC (DE, 1 AP = 4.66): Immobilized (EoT) 4.7, Slowed 2 3.6, Hindered (EoT) 1.5, Immobilized until it escapes 1.5, Slowed 1 1.4, Prone 1.2, Pulled 2 1.2, Slid 2 1.0, Pushed 1 to 4 0.8-1.1 (distance hardly matters), Hindered until it moves 0.7, Vulnerable 1 0.6 (E1 assumed 1.23). (4) **The Bestiary's condition steps, checked on PCs (proposed for E3):** Prone and Slowed 1 are worth about 3 steps (written Moderate, 2), Slowed 2 about 6 (Severe, 3), Pushed 3+ about 1 (Moderate, 2), Immobilized until it escapes about 2 (Severe, 3), Hindered 0 to 1 (Mild, 1): AP loss is the expensive kind, movement the cheap one. (5) **Opportunity Attack (the P1 Paladin's level 1 Talent) is worth 0**: the Paladin spends 5 AP every turn and keeping 1 costs 1.8 DE a turn (Options step). (6) **Speed is worth nothing on an open field** (±1 Speed: 0 to 0.07 DE a turn); it needs walls and Difficult Terrain, not modeled. (7) **Verdicts now final** (catalogue/archer.txt): Rolling Shot 1 over (1.65: its Evade saves the step away on 47% of the Archer's turns), Rolling Shot 2 under (0.88), Lock & Loose a little over (1.34), Wing Clip over (1.71), Pinning Shot over (2.19), True Shot under (0.07), Vanish under (0.47), Volley and Deadeye under, Perfect Shot on target (16% of a turn; it was priced 0 by a catalogue bug, fixed); Paladin: Bulwark of Faith 2R under (0.74 with Slid 2 = 1.0), Word of Radiance on target, Judgment a little over, Hand of Mercy on target (ending a Moderate condition = 4.7). Still not modeled: the Boon on Saves (NPC Saves), hazardous areas (Rain of Arrows, Covering Fire). **Assumptions I made** (all in map.mjs `MAP` and README): an open field with no walls; the fight starts 8 squares apart, the Archers 2 behind and to the sides; the map's own cover on 20% of the shots (5% Medium, 15% Low); only Elites have an Opportunity Attack; an NPC's Bestiary Strikes keep 1 AP for moving; an NPC prefers the PC it can strike most, and a hurt one a little more; the Archer's `when engaged` turn steps away (Advance, or Evade 1 next to an Elite). **Update (Lucas, 2026-10-29): Talents are left out of the class builds** (classes are compared with classes; a Talent is priced on its own: catalogue/, positions.mjs). Out: Opportunity Attack (P1 Paladin L1) and Heavy Blows (L2 stand-in); the Archer files had none. Level 1 doesn't change (Opportunity Attack was worth 0). Without Heavy Blows the class alone grows **0% from level 1 to 2** (Smite Paladin real sustained 81% → 71% of the baseline, 74% on the charter's curve; it took its Encounter Pick at level 1, so level 2 is only the Talent) and +11% to level 3 (Radiant Zeal); the Archer +8% at level 2 (Wing Clip). **Open for Lucas:** is the charter's +10% a level curve for the class alone, or for class + Talents (the charter says the growth comes from the Talents)?

Price 27% of a turn (0 AP; the guide says 8-25%). Lay On Hands' level 2-3 Nova waits for the Options step. **P1 is done**; the P1 party is now well above the Bestiary (E3).

- [ ] **P1, THE PALADIN MAIN BUILD (pack P1, 2026-10-29): PROPOSED, waiting for Lucas's approval; not in rules-src yet.** The cards, prices, budget table, changes and assumptions: `book/paladin-p1.md`; ruler files `book/ruler/classes/paladin-smite-*.txt`, `paladin-hands-*.txt`, `catalogue/paladin-p1.txt`. **Proposed:** level list L1 = 2 Features + 1 Signature (Smite *or* Lay On Hands) + 1 Talent + the Encounter Pick + 1 Passive and 1 Active Escalation; L2 = 1 Talent; L3 = 1 Daily + 1 Passive Escalation. Mind Health ×4. Features: Divine Power (the Signature choice; Profane Judgment folded in) and **Font of Mercy** (new: Rally Points used within 5 squares heal 2 more). Smite 2/3: 1d6 / 1d10 / 2d8 Holy, +1 Defense to you or an ally in the light (2 AP), and a 1d8 heal (3 AP); no Boon on the next Divine Action, no Appeal Save. Lay On Hands 2 AP: the next Strike this turn +[M] 2d8; a 3 AP version (2 Rally Points). **Holy Wrath** (new Encounter, 2/3 AP, the Nova). **Divine Surge** to allies (Lucas's decision, written: the next ally who damages the target before your next turn deals +[M] 1d8). Defaults kept: Hallowed Stance, Opportunity Attack, Consecrated Ground. New: Radiant Zeal (L3 passive, Escalation 1: the first Strike each round +1d6), Heavy Blows (a stand-in Talent, +1 damage on two-handed melee hits). **Numbers (level 1):** sustained 86-87% (was 109%), Nova 151-161% (was 104%), Health 124% (was 114%), support 18-27% of a turn, growth +10% a level (Smite); spend Divine Surge or hold for Hallowed Stance: even in DE, holding survives better. **Findings:** (1) a once-a-fight Nova is a third of a PC's turns (fights last about 3 rounds), so over a fight the Paladin deals 111-113% of the baseline: the budgets' "sustained" is read here as the turns without the Nova (Question 1 in paladin-p1.md); (2) Lay On Hands' Nova is 142% at L2-3; (3) proposed reprices for the Options step: Shield of Faith as a ward Reaction (1.12, was 0.19), Aura of Protection lasting the encounter (1.39, was 0.46); Champion's Challenge needs NPC targeting (E2); (4) the P1 party loses 13-22% of its Health in a CV 4 fight (target 40%): the Bestiary refit (E3) must follow. **The ruler now** simulates support (Divine Surge credit, new Health, areas with Banes and healing, Hallowed Stance on an adjacent ally, Font of Mercy), plan fallbacks (`A | B`), holding Escalation (`(keep 2)`), Dailies kept for a hard fight, and splits Nova turns from sustained turns; econ.mjs prices every catalogue and every class file, with a frozen Health baseline and the charter's growth curve.
- [ ] **CLASS CHARTER (Lucas 2026-10-29; book/class-charter.md, applied in the class rewrite, not in the class documents yet):** classes are written after the system; **Talent-based**: a class owns only its fantasy (2 Features, a Signature Class Action, role tools, its Escalation, Encounter and Daily lists), Talents own everything generic ("if two classes could want it, it is a Talent"); no "pick any class Action": more class options only through Talents (e.g. *Class Expertise*, a level 5 Talent: another Daily from your class). **Signature Class Action:** a choice of two (Paladin: Smite or Lay On Hands), each covering the whole role with a small rider from the other side. **Levels:** L1: 2 Features, 1 Signature, 1 Talent, 1 Passive and 1 Active Escalation; L2: 1 Encounter Pick, 1 Talent; L3: 1 Daily Pick, 1 Escalation Pick. Growth about +10% sustained a level (proposed; the Bestiary follows). **Momentum:** +1 after each fight harder than easy; with Momentum you start a fight with ⌈level ÷ 2⌉ Escalation, up to 2; a Rest resets it. Overlaps to fix: Aim (Talent) and Precise Aim, Opportunity Attack and Quick Draw. **Answered by Lucas the same day:** Momentum counts fights harder than easy, and 1 Momentum is enough; the level 3 Escalation Pick is Passive; the level list is a template (a class can move Picks between levels: the Paladin starts with an Encounter Pick); the Paladin is rewritten first, the Archer after; Momentum only sets the starting Escalation (abilities can read it: "When you start combat with 2 Escalation...").

With it, the Archer's real damage is within its budget (107% at L1, 100% at L2) and the Paladin's is over (117%, 106%; budget 85-90%); the "131% / 119%" of the first E1 run were wrong.

- [x] **Core Rules change log v2 APPLIED (2026-10-23).** Chapter 3 is now "Movement and the Environment": Playing on the Grid, Size (Squeezing; the cube/Elevation merged), Movement (Advance, Evade, Moving and Leaving Your Square, Corners), Straight Line, Forced Movement (Pushed, Pulled, Slid, Collision), Reposition (not Forced Movement; a path; no Reach or Size limit; needs Line of Effect; comparison table), Difficult Terrain, Hazardous Terrain, Reach, Engaged (Ranged Combat While Engaged), Flanking (threat = a Melee weapon you can use for the Weapon Attack; new weapon property **Unarmed**, Fists have it, Brawler included; six examples with the figures split in three), Line of Effect, Cover, Areas. Collision counts the unused squares of the destination the origin chose (the origin may always move less); Pulled may end next to the origin or aim at its square; Slid and Reposition need the origin's Line of Effect (losing it ends the movement, no damage); a solid corner = a wall or solid object touching the corner POINT; Forced Movement and Reposition never squeeze. Also: Rules Language has Origin and "Since your last turn / this turn"; Ranged banes are for [Ranged] Actions (not only Weapon); Ranged trait and Thrown wording match; Minor enemies = Minions and Grunts (Bestiary, heading "Minor enemies"); 7 new glossary entries; five new diagrams (Pulled, Slid, Reposition, Corners, Squeezing); the Pushed diagram's colors now match (O you = blue, G the goblin = red). The record is archive/book-drafts/changelog-core-v2-applied.txt.
- [x] **Handle Actions, SR, Steadiness, Immobilized and Fists APPLIED (2026-10-23).** **SR** (Size Rank) in the Size section and the Requirements (Shove, Relocate, Haul, Trip). The Handle Actions share a ladder with three versions: **1A: No effect / 1 / 2, 2A: No effect / 2 / 4, 3A: 1 / 3 / 5**: **Shove** (Pushed; 2A Tier 1 is now No effect), **Relocate** (the old Reposition Action, renamed; Reposition; the target must remain within your Reach), **Haul** (new; Pulled; needs Reach 2+, the target at least 2 squares away); all with Strike. **Steadiness** (a section after Reposition): reduces the value of Forced Movement and Reposition before the origin chooses; you can choose not to apply it; Human 0, Dwarf 1, Elf 0; NPCs 0 unless they say otherwise (Bestiary). **Immobilized** also stops Forced Movement and Reposition: no roll = disrupted; a roll or a Save Action must beat the Immobilized DC (11 + the Modifier the origin used; 13 without origin; the highest of several); one roll per Save Action. **Fists** are an ordinary weapon (Barehanded, Melee, Non-threatening; Range 1): while you have a free hand they count as a weapon you wield; the Fists card has the Weapon trait; the weapon property **Non-threatening** (renamed from Unarmed) means no flanking unless a feature says otherwise, and **Brawler** removes it ("you can flank with them"). Pummel's Critical says Relocate. Rules Language's "Specific beats general" example is now the Evade Action. 3 glossary entries changed or added (SR, Steadiness, Non-threatening). The record: archive/book-drafts/changelog-handle-actions-applied.txt.

diagram). **Light:** same shapes as areas (Bright light N = an N Emanation, Dim the same beyond; diagram redone), and light only matters where the TARGET is (Dim: Obscured; Darkness: Hidden, or Unsensed if you perceive it no other way): the penalties for standing in Dim light or Darkness yourself are gone (a rules change). **Class documents (Archer and Paladin alike):** no tagline, no title block (also gone from Species, Gear and Talents: builder rule; no "(Draft)" in those titles), `Level 1` section, `1st Level Picks`, the Repertoire by Frequency (Passive: Long Draw / Aegis, Mercy's Reward; Turn: Quick Draw / Rebuke), all the intro lines gone. **Archer cards:** Marked Quarry "you can choose a new Marked Quarry (0R [Focus])"; Suppressing Fire [Ranged]; Twin Shot (a Ranged Weapon Technique or Weapon Attack, extra [B] 1d6 Pi to each of the two); Perfect Shot [Ranged, Shot, Strike]. **KEPT for now (Lucas): Marked Quarry and Sharpshooter** (Marked Quarry is worth about 7% of the Archer's damage; Hunter's Focus still to change). **Still to do:** a level-by-level explanation of each class; the Archer design session; Momentum, Skills, Magic, Mastery and Gear.
- [x] **LEVELS: RULER, BESTIARY ROWS and RALLY POINTS APPLIED (2026-10-24).** Ruler by level: ruler/levels.mjs and its README. **Bestiary:** levels 1 to 3 are filled; level 4 waits for PC levels; the scaling: level 1 Basic attack damage is raised by half of the rating's bump (Terrible +2.5%, Low +3.75%, Medium +5%, High +6.25%, Extreme +7.5%), and from level 2 Health and damage grow like the PCs for Medium and by the rating's gap to Medium for the others; Attributes, Defenses and level 1 Health are unchanged (ruler/ratings.py: BUMP, dfac, hfac). **FIXED (Lucas: yes): the scaling is centered on Medium** (ruler/ratings.py: rel, dfac, hfac): a Medium NPC keeps exact pace with the PCs, so its fight stays at 1.05 of the old level 1 design at every level (the 5% margin: the PCs are a little ahead); Terrible 1.02 / 0.98 / 0.93, Low 1.04 / 1.01 / 0.99, High 1.06 / 1.09 / 1.11, Extreme 1.07 / 1.12 / 1.17 at levels 1 / 2 / 3 (a rating differs from Medium by (1 + its bump) / (1 + Medium's bump) a level: about 2% to 5%). A Medium Standard: damage 4.5/8, 5/8.5, 5.5/9.5, Health 50, 56, 56; an Extreme Standard: damage 7/11.5, 7.5/12.5, 8.5/14, Health 71, 80, 83. **Rally Points:** your class's Rally value + ceil((Body + Mind + level) / 2), each point removes ceil(Pool / 4); Paladin 6, Archer 5 (1.6 to 2.4 Health Pools a day at level 1, 1.9 to 2.5 at level 3, 2.2 to 2.8 at level 5; the Elf Archer is the lowest); the "plentiful on purpose" box is gone (9 open boxes).

- [x] (applied: see the line above) **NEW CORE RULE (Lucas, decided in principle): Ward Defense is higher by default.** Ward Defense = 11 + the Power Modifier (the one a class uses for Power rolls) + Ward value (items and features only: holy symbols, Warded armor, features) + bonuses. **The species Ward value goes away** (Human 1, Dwarf 1, Elf 2 are dropped, in the book and in Foundry). Reason: with NPC Ward Defense 11 against Armor Defense 13, Power Actions deal about 20% more than Weapon Actions (each point of Defense is about 10% of an attacker's damage), which made Power Actions the meta. Armor Defense stays 11 + Armor value (0 to 3) + bonuses; Lucas does not plan Attributes above 4 (maximum without items: Ward Defense 15 by his count). **OPEN, ask first: Lucas counted 15 = 11 + 4, which is 11 + the Attribute's value; "11 + Power Modifier" gives 14 at Attribute 4 (Modifier = ceil(4 / 2) + 1 = 3), and at level 1 almost everyone has Power Modifier +2 (Ward Defense 13). Which does he mean?** Consequences accepted by Lucas: martial characters with low Mind or Appeal are easier to hit with Power Actions (counter-play), and Armor Defense does not grow with levels like Ward Defense does (fine: Attributes stop at 4). Edit: book (Defenses, Building a Character, Species), Foundry (data.deriveDefenses, species ward field and compendium, WD tile hover, tests t129 and the Ward tests).
